It establishes instead that Gaussian smoothing belongs to a different proxy geometry. That is a respectable and potentially important boundary result, provided the paper states the distinction exactly.

2. Is the hypothesis set genuinely minimal and abstract?
2.1 The decisive objection: (K3) is redundant
The manuscript says that (K3) has a separate proof function and that the three conditions are the â€œexact inputs.â€  That is not correct.
Condition (K1), already at first order, gives
L=âˆ¥âˆ‡logpâˆ¥âˆžâ€‹<âˆž.
After changing variables in the translate entropy,
Hpâ€‹(z)=âˆ«Rdâ€‹p(w)logp(w+z)p(w)â€‹dw.
The fundamental theorem of calculus yields
âˆ£logp(w+z)âˆ’logp(w)âˆ£â‰¤Lâˆ£zâˆ£.
Therefore

Thus (K1) implies (K3), with the stronger linear-growth estimate.
The proof does use finite translate entropy, but it does not need it as an independent hypothesis. This is a real minimality failure, not merely a stylistic preference.
Required remedy: delete (K3) from the definition and insert:

Lemma (bounded score implies finite translate entropy).
If p>0, pâˆˆC1, and âˆ¥âˆ‡logpâˆ¥âˆžâ€‹â‰¤L, then

for every zâˆˆRd.

The theorem then becomes strictly stronger: only (K1) and the vanishing part of (K2) are needed.
If the authors wish to retain an entropy hypothesis in anticipation of variants without a bounded score, they must weaken or separate the first-order part of (K1). Keeping both assumptions and calling them independent or exact is indefensible.

Let

2.3 The remaining assumptions are natural, but not logically minimal in every possible proof architecture
After deleting (K3), the theorem gives a clean and useful sufficient kernel criterion. I would not call even the resulting two-condition package globally minimal.
For this particular Lâˆž, positive-additive-proxy proof:


bounded lower-order normalized derivatives keep the common retained jet uniformly small and hence preserve proxy positivity;


bounded top modes make the quadratic perturbation bounded;


the bounded score supplies the uniform lower background;


quotient continuity supplies the o(âˆ£zâˆ£r) local remainder.


These are genuine proof functions.
But one could weaken the theorem by replacing them with more modular assumptions directly on:


boundedness and zero mass of the modes;


an Lâˆžâˆ©L1 Peano remainder;


a law-dependent or kernel-dependent minorization of the actual quotients;


finite entropy of exterior mixtures.


That would be a weaker but more tautological abstract theorem. The present kernel-level formulation is preferable because it is verifiable. It should be called a clean sufficient kernel package, not the uniquely minimal or exact hypothesis set.
2.4 The abstraction is nevertheless genuine
The proof really has shed stability, regular variation, semigroup structure, radial symmetry, and isotropy. The local quotient expansion, raw-tail retention, entropy transfer, and Fourier positivity argument use none of those structures. 
The Student family is also a meaningful verification. For Î²î€ =d+1, those kernels are neither Poisson kernels nor stable kernels, and the derivative and entropy estimates are verified directly from their rational form.  Consequently, this is not merely Theorem 4.27 with the words â€œstable densityâ€ replaced by axioms.
There is, however, less diversity than the list â€œstable, Poisson, Studentâ€ initially suggests:


Poisson is already the Î±=1 stable case.


All presently verified examples are radial polynomial-tail kernels.


The Student family provides the one genuinely independent family.


That is enough to defeat the charge that the theorem is built around one exact kernel. It is not quite enough to make the breadth of the abstraction visually undeniable.
2.5 One additional class would materially improve the theorem
A particularly easy and genuinely distinct example is the product logistic family
p(x)=j=1âˆdâ€‹4cosh2(xjâ€‹/2)1â€‹.
In one dimension, every normalized derivative p(k)/p is a polynomial in tanh(x/2), hence bounded. The next normalized derivative gives the quotient modulus, and the bounded score gives translate entropy. Tensor products preserve these properties.
This would provide simultaneously:


exponential rather than polynomial tails;


a nonradial multidimensional kernel;


no stable or semigroup interpretation;


a very short verification.


Alternatively, the paper could prove closure under invertible linear images, tensor products, and finite positive mixtures satisfying common normalized-derivative bounds. Even one such closure proposition would demonstrate that the class is structurally populated rather than consisting of a hand-picked list.
3. Does the Gaussian exclusion satisfy the requested development?
Literal answer: no
The requested development was a theorem encompassing stable, Gaussian, Poisson, and other polynomial-tail kernels. The present theorem does not encompass the Gaussian. Therefore it does not literally deliver that theorem.
Gaussian first-unmatched-moment KL asymptotics under stronger tail assumptions are already available through the Hermite/Gaussian mechanism, as the manuscript correctly acknowledges.  Chen and Niles-Weedâ€™s theorem gives precisely the Gaussian KL coefficient in terms of the first unmatched moments under their exponential-moment hypothesis. arXiv
Substantive answer: the boundary theorem is a legitimate replacement
The failure is not an accidental inability to verify one estimate. It occurs at the defining geometric step of the construction:


normalized Gaussian derivatives are unbounded;


translate quotients have no uniform lower bound;


the prescribed additive common jet can become negative.


That establishes that the positive additive tail-proxy construction and the Hermite/Gaussian construction are genuinely different mechanisms. The manuscript is mathematically better for saying this than it would be if it concealed Gaussian failure in an artificial tail assumption.
The correct positioning is therefore:

The theorem abstracts the bounded-score positive additive tail-proxy mechanism.
Gaussian smoothing lies on the other side of its sharp structural boundary and requires a different, typically weighted or multiplicative Hermite mechanism.

The paper should not say that it completed the earlier â€œstableâ€“Gaussianâ€“Poissonâ€ umbrella theorem. It should say that it obtained a broad abstract theorem and proved why the Gaussian cannot belong to that theorem without changing the proxy architecture.
What would literally include Gaussian?
A genuine common umbrella would probably need two branches:


a bounded-score/additive branch, containing stable, Poisson, Student, logistic, and similar kernels;


an unbounded-score/multiplicative or exponential-jet branch, containing the Gaussian and using weighted Hermite control.


Such a theorem would have a common conclusion but different positivity mechanisms. It would also almost certainly impose stronger assumptions on Gaussian inputs, because Gaussian translate quotients grow exponentially and finite r-th moments alone do not provide the exterior entropy control used here.
I would regard that as a further paper-level advance, not as a necessary repair to the present result.
Does the Gaussian boundary cap the paper?
It caps the universality of this particular mechanism. It does not cap the paper at a low mathematical tier. A sharp impossibility boundary can strengthen an abstract theorem, especially when it explains why a major classical example requires a different method.
The boundary must, however, be advertised as a mechanism boundary, not merely as â€œGaussian is not admissible under our definition.â€
4. The Gaussian counterexample
The counterexample is correct.
For

Their second moments differ by a2, as required for an order-two first mismatch.
For the standard one-dimensional Gaussian,
ggâ€²â€‹=âˆ’y,

Exactly what the example proves
It proves that:


the prescribed additive common-jet proxy is not positive for all moment-matched Gaussian pairs;


the uniform positive-background interface used by the two-background lemma is unavailable in this architecture;


the Gaussian is not an instance of the theorem by a superficial change of estimates.


It does not prove that:


Gaussian first-unmatched-moment asymptotics fail;


no positive Gaussian proxy can be constructed;


no multiplicative or exponentially normalized proxy can work;


every possible Gaussian tail-defect decomposition is impossible.


The manuscript expressly avoids those overclaims, which is correct.
I would revise one phrase. Saying that â€œthe bounded cubic remainder failsâ€ sounds broader than what the example directly establishes. The counterexample shows that the bounded-mode and positive-background hypotheses needed for the manuscriptâ€™s cubic estimate fail. A safer formulation is:

â€œConsequently, the uniformly bounded cubic-remainder estimate in the two-background additive-jet expansion is unavailable.â€

That says exactly what has been proved.
5. Centrality of the mechanism and venue judgment
Mathematically, the mechanism is now central
Taken together, the manuscript now has:


the arbitrary-order stable law-by-law decomposition;


a sufficient condition for defect vanishing;


examples where the scaled defect diverges, proving non-vacuousness;


robustness under a substantial fixed-scale convention class;


coefficient rigidity within the specified raw-tail/common-core ansatz;


an abstract kernel theorem;


nonstable verified kernels;


a sharp Gaussian mechanism boundary.


The non-vacuousness statement shows that the proxy term is not a formal remainder that always disappears under the theoremâ€™s minimal moment assumption.  The robustness theorem shows that the criterion and Hessian coefficient survive changes of cutoffs, gauges, and the two specified normalizations.  The rigidity statement identifies precisely what is forced inside the stated ansatz and carefully disclaims global canonicity. 
That is enough for the mechanism, rather than a single stable-kernel implementation, to be a principal contribution.
Structurally, the manuscript has not yet made it central enough
The most general theorem is currently unnumbered, and it is followed by a separately numbered stable theorem whose proof substantially repeats the same architecture. The abstract theorem occupies the position of an inserted generalization rather than the position of the theorem organizing the section. 
I would require the following reorganization:


Number and promote the abstract theorem as a main theorem.


State the stable result as an immediate corollary or exact specialization.


Remove duplicated proof material from Theorem 4.27, retaining only the identification of notation and any stable-specific consequences.


Put the kernel-verification proposition immediately after the abstract theorem.


Put the Gaussian boundary in a separate proposition or theorem, not merely in the latter part of a verification proposition.


That would make the logical hierarchy visible:
abstractÂ mechanismâŸ¹stable/Poisson/Student/logisticÂ instancesâŸ¹stable-specificÂ sharpnessÂ andÂ non-vacuousness.
At present, the abstract and introduction already describe the mechanism as a major contribution, including its robustness and rigidity.  The body should reflect the same hierarchy.
Remaining JFA-level concerns
The mathematical case for a JFA submission is now credible. I would nevertheless not recommend submission of the current version without the following repairs:


Delete (K3) or stop claiming minimality.
My strong preference is deletion, because (K1) proves the stronger estimate.


Change â€œexact inputsâ€ to â€œa sufficient kernel criterionâ€ unless a formal necessity theorem is added.


Simplify (K2) by removing its redundant finiteness clause.


Add one genuinely different verified class, preferably product logistic kernels or a closure theorem covering affine images, products, and finite mixtures.


Narrow the Gaussian cubic-remainder wording to the particular additive two-background estimate.


Promote and number the abstract theorem, making the stable theorem a specialization rather than a second primary proof.


Control the manuscriptâ€™s breadth.
The paper contains several substantial arcsâ€”Cayley coefficient algebra, sharp stable moment exponents, the positive-proxy mechanism, Bregman identities, and RKHS/lattice results. Either the introduction must impose a very clear hierarchy among them, or some of the later RKHS material should move to a companion paper or supplementary section. This is now a more serious venue risk than the correctness of the proxy theorem.


Final venue judgment
After the changes above, a JFA submission is defensible and mathematically credible. I would no longer describe it as a speculative reach based on one successful stable construction.
I would not predict secure acceptance. Priority evaluation, the manuscriptâ€™s breadth, and the editorâ€™s view of whether the several analytic arcs form one paper remain genuine risks. But I would not recommend rejection on the ground that the abstract mechanism is hollow or mathematically incorrect.
My present assessment can be summarized as follows:

The strongest objection is therefore not a hidden analytic failure. It is a correctable overstatement about the hypothesis package. Removing that overstatementâ€”and in fact removing the redundant hypothesisâ€”would leave a stronger abstract theorem than the one presently advertised.

---
name: using-superpowers
description: Use when starting any conversation - establishes how to find and use skills, requiring Skill tool invocation before ANY response including clarifying questions
---

<SUBAGENT-STOP>
If you were dispatched as a subagent to execute a specific task, skip this skill.
</SUBAGENT-STOP>

<EXTREMELY-IMPORTANT>
If you think there is even a 1% chance a skill might apply to what you are doing, you ABSOLUTELY MUST invoke the skill.

IF A SKILL APPLIES TO YOUR TASK, YOU DO NOT HAVE A CHOICE. YOU MUST USE IT.

This is not negotiable. This is not optional. You cannot rationalize your way out of this.
</EXTREMELY-IMPORTANT>

## Instruction Priority

Superpowers skills override default system prompt behavior, but **user instructions always take precedence**:

1. **User's explicit instructions** (CLAUDE.md, GEMINI.md, AGENTS.md, direct requests) â€” highest priority
2. **Superpowers skills** â€” override default system behavior where they conflict
3. **Default system prompt** â€” lowest priority

If CLAUDE.md, GEMINI.md, or AGENTS.md says "don't use TDD" and a skill says "always use TDD," follow the user's instructions. The user is in control.

## How to Access Skills

**In Claude Code:** Use the `Skill` tool. When you invoke a skill, its content is loaded and presented to youâ€”follow it directly. Never use the Read tool on skill files.

**In Copilot CLI:** Use the `skill` tool. Skills are auto-discovered from installed plugins. The `skill` tool works the same as Claude Code's `Skill` tool.

**In Gemini CLI:** Skills activate via the `activate_skill` tool. Gemini loads skill metadata at session start and activates the full content on demand.

**In other environments:** Check your platform's documentation for how skills are loaded.

## Platform Adaptation

Skills use Claude Code tool names. Non-CC platforms: see `references/copilot-tools.md` (Copilot CLI), `references/codex-tools.md` (Codex) for tool equivalents. Gemini CLI users get the tool mapping loaded automatically via GEMINI.md.

# Using Skills

## The Rule

**Invoke relevant or requested skills BEFORE any response or action.** Even a 1% chance a skill might apply means that you should invoke the skill to check. If an invoked skill turns out to be wrong for the situation, you don't need to use it.

```dot
digraph skill_flow {
    "User message received" [shape=doublecircle];
    "About to EnterPlanMode?" [shape=doublecircle];
    "Already brainstormed?" [shape=diamond];
    "Invoke brainstorming skill" [shape=box];
    "Might any skill apply?" [shape=diamond];
    "Invoke Skill tool" [shape=box];
    "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'" [shape=box];
    "Has checklist?" [shape=diamond];
    "Create TodoWrite todo per item" [shape=box];
    "Follow skill exactly" [shape=box];
    "Respond (including clarifications)" [shape=doublecircle];

    "About to EnterPlanMode?" -> "Already brainstormed?";
    "Already brainstormed?" -> "Invoke brainstorming skill" [label="no"];
    "Already brainstormed?" -> "Might any skill apply?" [label="yes"];
    "Invoke brainstorming skill" -> "Might any skill apply?";

    "User message received" -> "Might any skill apply?";
    "Might any skill apply?" -> "Invoke Skill tool" [label="yes, even 1%"];
    "Might any skill apply?" -> "Respond (including clarifications)" [label="definitely not"];
    "Invoke Skill tool" -> "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'";
    "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'" -> "Has checklist?";
    "Has checklist?" -> "Create TodoWrite todo per item" [label="yes"];
    "Has checklist?" -> "Follow skill exactly" [label="no"];
    "Create TodoWrite todo per item" -> "Follow skill exactly";
}
```

## Red Flags

These thoughts mean STOPâ€”you're rationalizing:

| Thought | Reality |
|---------|---------|
| "This is just a simple question" | Questions are tasks. Check for skills. |
| "I need more context first" | Skill check comes BEFORE clarifying questions. |
| "Let me explore the codebase first" | Skills tell you HOW to explore. Check first. |
| "I can check git/files quickly" | Files lack conversation context. Check for skills. |
| "Let me gather information first" | Skills tell you HOW to gather information. |
| "This doesn't need a formal skill" | If a skill exists, use it. |
| "I remember this skill" | Skills evolve. Read current version. |
| "This doesn't count as a task" | Action = task. Check for skills. |
| "The skill is overkill" | Simple things become complex. Use it. |
| "I'll just do this one thing first" | Check BEFORE doing anything. |
| "This feels productive" | Undisciplined action wastes time. Skills prevent this. |
| "I know what that means" | Knowing the concept â‰  using the skill. Invoke it. |

## Skill Priority

When multiple skills could apply, use this order:

1. **Process skills first** (brainstorming, debugging) - these determine HOW to approach the task
2. **Implementation skills second** (frontend-design, mcp-builder) - these guide execution

"Let's build X" â†’ brainstorming first, then implementation skills.
"Fix this bug" â†’ debugging first, then domain-specific skills.

## Skill Types

**Rigid** (TDD, debugging): Follow exactly. Don't adapt away discipline.

**Flexible** (patterns): Adapt principles to context.

The skill itself tells you which.

## User Instructions

Instructions say WHAT, not HOW. "Add X" or "Fix Y" doesn't mean skip workflows.

name: verification-before-completion
description: Use when about to claim work is complete, fixed, or passing, before committing or creating PRs - requires running verification commands and confirming output before making any success claims; evidence before assertions always
---

# Verification Before Completion

## Overview

Claiming work is complete without verification is dishonesty, not efficiency.

**Core principle:** Evidence before claims, always.

**Violating the letter of this rule is violating the spirit of this rule.**

## The Iron Law

```
NO COMPLETION CLAIMS WITHOUT FRESH VERIFICATION EVIDENCE
```

If you haven't run the verification command in this message, you cannot claim it passes.

## The Gate Function

```
BEFORE claiming any status or expressing satisfaction:

1. IDENTIFY: What command proves this claim?
2. RUN: Execute the FULL command (fresh, complete)
3. READ: Full output, check exit code, count failures
4. VERIFY: Does output confirm the claim?
   - If NO: State actual status with evidence
   - If YES: State claim WITH evidence
5. ONLY THEN: Make the claim

Skip any step = lying, not verifying
```

## Common Failures

| Claim | Requires | Not Sufficient |
|-------|----------|----------------|
| Tests pass | Test command output: 0 failures | Previous run, "should pass" |
| Linter clean | Linter output: 0 errors | Partial check, extrapolation |
| Build succeeds | Build command: exit 0 | Linter passing, logs look good |
| Bug fixed | Test original symptom: passes | Code changed, assumed fixed |
| Regression test works | Red-green cycle verified | Test passes once |
| Agent completed | VCS diff shows changes | Agent reports "success" |
| Requirements met | Line-by-line checklist | Tests passing |

## Red Flags - STOP

- Using "should", "probably", "seems to"
- Expressing satisfaction before verification ("Great!", "Perfect!", "Done!", etc.)
- About to commit/push/PR without verification
- Trusting agent success reports
- Relying on partial verification
- Thinking "just this once"
- Tired and wanting work over
- **ANY wording implying success without having run verification**

## Rationalization Prevention

| Excuse | Reality |
|--------|---------|
| "Should work now" | RUN the verification |
| "I'm confident" | Confidence â‰  evidence |
| "Just this once" | No exceptions |
| "Linter passed" | Linter â‰  compiler |
| "Agent said success" | Verify independently |
| "I'm tired" | Exhaustion â‰  excuse |
| "Partial check is enough" | Partial proves nothing |
| "Different words so rule doesn't apply" | Spirit over letter |

## Key Patterns

**Tests:**
```
âœ… [Run test command] [See: 34/34 pass] "All tests pass"
âŒ "Should pass now" / "Looks correct"
```

**Regression tests (TDD Red-Green):**
```
âœ… Write â†’ Run (pass) â†’ Revert fix â†’ Run (MUST FAIL) â†’ Restore â†’ Run (pass)
âŒ "I've written a regression test" (without red-green verification)
```

**Build:**
```
âœ… [Run build] [See: exit 0] "Build passes"
âŒ "Linter passed" (linter doesn't check compilation)
```

**Requirements:**
```
âœ… Re-read plan â†’ Create checklist â†’ Verify each â†’ Report gaps or completion
âŒ "Tests pass, phase complete"
```

**Agent delegation:**
```
âœ… Agent reports success â†’ Check VCS diff â†’ Verify changes â†’ Report actual state
âŒ Trust agent report
```

## Why This Matters

From 24 failure memories:
- your human partner said "I don't believe you" - trust broken
- Undefined functions shipped - would crash
- Missing requirements shipped - incomplete features
- Time wasted on false completion â†’ redirect â†’ rework
- Violates: "Honesty is a core value. If you lie, you'll be replaced."

## When To Apply

**ALWAYS before:**
- ANY variation of success/completion claims
- ANY expression of satisfaction
- ANY positive statement about work state
- Committing, PR creation, task completion
- Moving to next task
- Delegating to agents

**Rule applies to:**
- Exact phrases
- Paraphrases and synonyms
- Implications of success
- ANY communication suggesting completion/correctness

## The Bottom Line

**No shortcuts for verification.**

Run the command. Read the output. THEN claim the result.

This is non-negotiable.

\title[Cayley--Poisson and Stable-Kernel Entropy]{Cayley--Poisson Entropy and
Optimal Stable-Kernel Moment Exponents}
\author{Anonymous author}
\date{}

\keywords{Cayley transform, Poisson semigroup, isotropic stable kernel,
relative entropy asymptotics, optimal moment exponent, Cayley--Chebyshev
modes, nonlocal Bregman dissipation, regular variation}
\subjclass[2020]{47D07, 60E10, 94A17, 42A38}

\begin{document}
\def\JFAArticleMain{1}
\begin{abstract}
Arbitrary-order first unmatched moment relative-entropy asymptotics under
Gaussian smoothing are due to Chen and Niles-Weed
\cite{ChenNilesWeed2022SmoothedWasserstein}, while all-order
stable/fractional heat-kernel density expansions are also prior work
\cite{IshigeKawakamiMichihisa2017,
IshigeKawakamiMichihisa2022RefinedFractional}.  Neither result is claimed as
new.  For the heavy-tailed isotropic strictly \(\alpha\)-stable kernels,
\(0<\alpha<2\), we identify the optimal complete-moment condition that
transfers the density expansion through relative entropy.
If two
laws on \(\RR^d\) match all tensor moments below the integer order \(r\ge1\),
have a nonzero order-\(r\) difference \(\Delta_r\), and have finite
\(p_{r,\alpha,d}\)th absolute moments, where
\[
 p_{r,\alpha,d}=\max\left\{r,
 \frac{2r(d+\alpha)}{d+\alpha+2r}\right\},
\]
then
\[
 D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
 =\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)s^{-2r}+o(s^{-2r}).
\]
The coefficient is an orthogonally invariant positive-definite quadratic
form on symmetric order-\(r\) tensors.  The endpoint moment is sufficient.
When \(d+\alpha>2r\), every exponent
\(\rho\in[r,p_{r,\alpha,d})\) fails uniformly even for pairs with finite
nonzero \(\Delta_r\); when \(d+\alpha\le2r\), the threshold equals \(r\), and
smaller exponents fail only to define \(\Delta_r\) throughout the moment
class.  Thus the contribution is the heavy-tailed critical exponent,
endpoint sufficiency, and uniform sharpness, not the Gaussian quadratic
mechanism itself.

At every order \(r\), finite \(r\)th moments alone also give a law-by-law
decomposition: the universal quadratic tensor term plus a nonnegative
relative \(\Phi\)-energy between two positive proxies that retain each law's
unexpanded tail beyond the smoothing scale and their common lower-order jet.
The mechanism needs neither stability nor regular variation: an abstract
kernel theorem isolates bounded normalized jets, quotient continuity of the
top retained derivative, and finite polynomial-growth translate entropy as
the exact inputs.  It applies to isotropic stable kernels, hence directly to
the Poisson kernel, and to every multivariate Student kernel.  Gaussian
smoothing lies outside this additive positive-proxy theorem because its
normalized derivatives are unbounded; the paper records the precise proxy
positivity failure rather than hiding it in a tail assumption.
To the best of our knowledge, this explicit positive proxy decomposition,
with vanishing of its KL term as an exact necessary and sufficient condition
for attainment of the universal coefficient, is new; no uniqueness or
canonicity of the proxy is asserted.  The defect is nevertheless unchanged
up to \(o(s^{-2r})\) by separate comparable hard or smooth retention profiles,
by either additive or multiplicative mass normalization, and by common
bounded order-\(r\) proxy gauges.  Among order-\(r\) jet-faithful proxies in
the prescribed retained-tail/common-core ansatz, asymptotic coefficient
rigidity identifies this gauge as the remaining freedom; no classification
of all reasonable proxy conventions is claimed.
For the Cauchy kernel, the Cayley
transform additionally yields the exact equivalence between finite variance
and bounded \(t^4\) relative entropy, an all-order finite Laurent rule for the
subsequent coefficients, and a fully justified nonlocal Bregman tail identity
for the moving matched quotient.
\end{abstract}

\subsection*{The positive tail-jet mechanism for an abstract kernel}

We now separate the kernel properties from their stable implementation.  Fix
an integer \(r\ge1\).  A strictly positive probability density
\(p\in C^r(\RR^d)\) will be called \emph{tail-jet admissible of order \(r\)}
if the following three conditions hold.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(K\arabic*)},leftmargin=2.8em]
\item For every multi-index \(\gamma\) with \(1\le|\gamma|\le r\),
\[
 \left\|\frac{\partial^\gamma p}{p}\right\|_\infty<\infty.
\]
\item The top normalized derivative has the quotient translation modulus
\[
 \omega_{p,r}(t):=
 \max_{|\gamma|=r}\ \sup_{|h|\le t}
 \left\|\frac{\partial^\gamma p(\mathord\cdot-h)
                   -\partial^\gamma p}{p}\right\|_\infty,
 \qquad 0<t\le1,
\]
finite for \(0<t\le1\) and satisfying
\(\omega_{p,r}(t)\to0\) as \(t\downarrow0\).
\item The translate entropy is finite and, for some \(C_{p,r}<\infty\),
\[
 \mathsf H_p(z):=
 D_{\rm KL}\bigl(p(\mathord\cdot-z)\|p\bigr)
 \le C_{p,r}(1+|z|^r),\qquad z\in\RR^d.
\]
\end{enumerate}
Put \(p_s(x)=s^{-d}p(x/s)\),
\(\Omega_p(\dd y)=p(y)\dd y\), and
\[
 F_y^p(z)=\frac{p(y-z)}{p(y)}.
\]
For a law \(\lambda\) with finite \(r\)th absolute moment define
\[
 B_{p,j}^\lambda(y):=
 \frac{(-1)^j}{p(y)}
 \sum_{|\gamma|=j}\frac{m_\gamma(\lambda)}{\gamma!}
 \partial^\gamma p(y),\qquad 1\le j\le r.
\]

\begin{unnumberedtheorem}[Abstract positive tail-jet kernel theorem]
Let \(p\) be tail-jet admissible of order \(r\), and let \(\nu,\eta\) be
probability laws on \(\RR^d\) such that
\[
 m_\gamma(\nu)=m_\gamma(\eta)\quad(|\gamma|<r),
 \qquad \int |x|^r(\nu+\eta)(\dd x)<\infty.
\]
For \(\lambda\in\{\nu,\eta\}\), set
\[
 \tau_s^\lambda=\lambda(|x|>s),\qquad
 V_{p,s}^\lambda(y)=
 \int_{|x|>s}F_y^p(x/s)\lambda(\dd x),
\]
and let the common retained jet and the two proxies be
\begin{align*}
 C_{p,r-1,s}
 &=\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}s^{-j}B_{p,j}^\nu
  =\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}s^{-j}B_{p,j}^\eta,\\
 A_{p,s}^\lambda
 &=1+C_{p,r-1,s}+V_{p,s}^\lambda-\tau_s^\lambda.
\end{align*}
The sum is empty when \(r=1\).  For all sufficiently large \(s\), both
\(A_{p,s}^\lambda\) are positive probability densities relative to
\(\Omega_p\).  Thus
\[
 k_{p,r,s}^\lambda(x)=s^{-d}p(x/s)A_{p,s}^\lambda(x/s)
\]
are probability densities and the finite, nonnegative proxy energy
\[
 \mathcal E_{p,r,s}(\nu,\eta)
 :=D_{\rm KL}(k_{p,r,s}^\nu\|k_{p,r,s}^\eta)
\]
is well defined.  If
\(\Delta_r=\int x^{\otimes r}(\nu-\eta)(\dd x)\), put
\[
 B_{p,\Delta_r}(y)=
 \frac{(-1)^r}{r!p(y)}\langle\Delta_r,D^rp(y)\rangle,
 \qquad
 \mathcal Q_{p,r}(\Delta_r)
 =\frac12\int B_{p,\Delta_r}^2\dd\Omega_p.
\]
Then
\[
 D_{\rm KL}(p_s*\nu\|p_s*\eta)
 =\mathcal Q_{p,r}(\Delta_r)s^{-2r}
  +\mathcal E_{p,r,s}(\nu,\eta)+o(s^{-2r}).
\]
Moreover \(\mathcal Q_{p,r}\) is finite and positive definite on
\(\operatorname{Sym}^r(\RR^d)\), and hence, law by law,
\[
 s^{2r}D_{\rm KL}(p_s*\nu\|p_s*\eta)
 \longrightarrow\mathcal Q_{p,r}(\Delta_r)
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 s^{2r}\mathcal E_{p,r,s}(\nu,\eta)\longrightarrow0.
\]
\end{unnumberedtheorem}

\begin{proof}
We give every kernel-dependent step.  Let
\[
 \mathsf B_{p,j}(y,z)=
 \frac{(-1)^j}{j!p(y)}\langle D^jp(y),z^{\otimes j}\rangle,
 \qquad \mathsf B_{p,0}=1.
\]
Condition \textup{(K1)} gives, for \(1\le j\le r\),
\[
 \|\mathsf B_{p,j}(\mathord\cdot,z)\|_\infty
 +\|\mathsf B_{p,j}(\mathord\cdot,z)\|_{L^1(\Omega_p)}
 \le C_{p,r}|z|^j.
\]
It also gives \(D^rp\in L^1\).  Consequently ordinary translation
continuity in \(L^1\), the integral Taylor formula, and \textup{(K2)} imply
that, for \(|z|\le1\),
\begin{align*}
 &\left\|F_\cdot^p(z)-\sum_{j=0}^r
       \mathsf B_{p,j}(\mathord\cdot,z)\right\|_\infty
 +\left\|F_\cdot^p(z)-\sum_{j=0}^r
       \mathsf B_{p,j}(\mathord\cdot,z)\right\|_{L^1(\Omega_p)}\\
 &\hspace{35mm}\le \varepsilon_{p,r}(|z|)|z|^r,
\end{align*}
where \(\varepsilon_{p,r}\) is bounded on \([0,1]\) and tends to zero at
zero.  Indeed, after subtracting the degree-\(r\) Taylor polynomial, the
uniform remainder is bounded by a constant times
\(|z|^r\omega_{p,r}(|z|)\).  After multiplication by \(p(y)\), the
\(L^1(\Omega_p)\) remainder is bounded by a constant times
\[
 |z|^r\max_{|\gamma|=r}\sup_{|h|\le|z|}
 \|\partial^\gamma p(\mathord\cdot-h)-\partial^\gamma p\|_1,
\]
which is \(o(|z|^r)\).  This proves the required local Taylor statement
without a derivative of order \(r+1\).

Define the actual quotient
\[
 q_{p,s}^\lambda(y)=\int F_y^p(x/s)\lambda(\dd x).
\]
Splitting at \(|x|=s\), retaining the exterior translate exactly, and using
the moments gives
\[
 q_{p,s}^\lambda
 =A_{p,s}^\lambda+s^{-r}B_{p,r}^\lambda+R_{p,s}^\lambda,
 \qquad
 \|R_{p,s}^\lambda\|_\infty
 +\|R_{p,s}^\lambda\|_{L^1(\Omega_p)}=o(s^{-r}).
\]
For completeness, after multiplication by \(s^r\) the interior Taylor
remainder is dominated by \(C|x|^r\) and converges pointwise to zero.  Every
discarded exterior mode satisfies
\[
 s^{-j}\int_{|x|>s}|x|^j\lambda(\dd x)
 \le s^{-r}\int_{|x|>s}|x|^r\lambda(\dd x)=o(s^{-r}),
 \qquad j\le r.
\]
The same tail-moment estimate gives \(\tau_s^\lambda=o(s^{-r})\).

Translation preserves mass.  Hence \(V_{p,s}^\lambda\ge0\),
\(\int V_{p,s}^\lambda\dd\Omega_p=\tau_s^\lambda\), and integration of
the distributional derivatives of the integrable density \(p\) gives
\(\int B_{p,j}^\lambda\dd\Omega_p=0\).  Therefore each proxy has mass one,
and
\[
 \inf_yA_{p,s}^\lambda(y)
 \ge1-\|C_{p,r-1,s}\|_\infty-\tau_s^\lambda\longrightarrow1.
\]
This is both proxy normalization and proxy positivity; no tail comparison is
used.

We next verify entropy finiteness.  When \(\tau_s^\lambda>0\), convexity of
relative entropy and \textup{(K3)} give, for the normalized exterior mixture
quotient \(Q_{p,s}^\lambda=V_{p,s}^\lambda/\tau_s^\lambda\),
\[
 \int Q_{p,s}^\lambda\log Q_{p,s}^\lambda\dd\Omega_p
 \le\frac{C_{p,r}}{\tau_s^\lambda}
 \int_{|x|>s}\{1+|x/s|^r\}\lambda(\dd x)<\infty.
\]
The elementary inequalities
\(\Phi(\tau q)\le\tau q\log(1+q)\) for \(0\le\tau\le1\), and
\[
 (u+v)\log^+(u+v)\le C\{1+v+\Phi(v)\},
 \qquad 0\le u\le3/2,\quad v\ge0,
\]
then show
\(\int A_{p,s}^\lambda\log^+A_{p,s}^\lambda\dd\Omega_p<\infty\).
The case \(\tau_s^\lambda=0\) is immediate.  Since the two proxies have a
common positive lower bound, their relative entropy is finite.

It remains to identify the quadratic term.  Condition \textup{(K1)} at
order one says \(\|\nabla\log p\|_\infty=L<\infty\).  Thus
\[
 F_y^p(z)\ge e^{-L|z|},\qquad
 q_{p,s}^\lambda(y)
 \ge e^{-L\int|x|\lambda(\dd x)/s}\longrightarrow1
\]
uniformly in \(y\).  The backgrounds tend to one in \(L^1(\Omega_p)\), and
their common jet cancels, so
\begin{align*}
 \|A_{p,s}^\lambda-1\|_1
 &\le\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}s^{-j}\|B_{p,j}^\lambda\|_1
      +2\tau_s^\lambda=o(1),\\
 \|A_{p,s}^\nu-A_{p,s}^\eta\|_1
 &\le2(\tau_s^\nu+\tau_s^\eta)=o(s^{-r}).
\end{align*}
All hypotheses of the two-background KL perturbation lemma are now
satisfied with \(\epsilon_s=s^{-r}\).  Its Hessian and cubic-remainder
calculation gives
\[
 D_{\rm KL}(p_s*\nu\|p_s*\eta)
 =\mathcal E_{p,r,s}(\nu,\eta)
  +\frac{s^{-2r}}2\int
       (B_{p,r}^\nu-B_{p,r}^\eta)^2\dd\Omega_p+o(s^{-2r}).
\]
The moment identity makes the difference in the integral exactly
\(B_{p,\Delta_r}\), proving the decomposition.

Finiteness of \(\mathcal Q_{p,r}\) follows from \textup{(K1)}.  If it
vanishes, then
\[
 \sum_{|\gamma|=r}\frac{\Delta m_\gamma}{\gamma!}
 \partial^\gamma p=0
\]
almost everywhere.  Fourier transformation gives
\(P(i\xi)\widehat p(\xi)=0\), where \(P\) is the corresponding homogeneous
polynomial.  Since \(\widehat p(0)=1\), continuity makes \(\widehat p\)
nonzero on a neighborhood of zero.  Thus \(P\) vanishes on an open set and
is identically zero.  Hence \(\Delta_r=0\), proving positive definiteness.
Nonnegativity of the proxy KL energy proves the final equivalence.
\end{proof}

The hypotheses above are arranged by proof function, not by tail class.
Condition \textup{(K1)} defines and bounds the retained modes, makes their
integrals vanish, and supplies the logarithmic-gradient lower bound.
Condition \textup{(K2)} is exactly the uniform little-\(o\) Taylor remainder;
the \(L^1\) counterpart is automatic because \textup{(K1)} puts \(D^rp\) in
\(L^1\).  Condition \textup{(K3)} is used only to make the retained-tail and
proxy entropies finite.  Neither decay, regular variation, a semigroup law,
radial symmetry, nor isotropy is assumed.  Isotropy merely adds orthogonal
invariance of \(\mathcal Q_{p,r}\).

\begin{unnumberedproposition}[Verified kernels and the Gaussian boundary]
For every order \(r\ge1\), the abstract theorem applies to each isotropic
strictly \(\alpha\)-stable density, \(0<\alpha<2\), to the Poisson kernel in
every dimension, and to every multivariate Student kernel
\[
 p_{\beta,d}(y)=c_{\beta,d}(1+|y|^2)^{-\beta/2},\qquad \beta>d.
\]
For the stable density, the specialization is exactly Theorem
\ref{thm:stable-arbitrary-order-law-by-law-decomposition}, with identical
proxies, energy, and quadratic form.  The standard Gaussian density is not
tail-jet admissible: \textup{(K1)} and \textup{(K2)} fail, and the additive
retained common jet need not be positive.  Thus the abstract theorem makes no
Gaussian claim.
\end{unnumberedproposition}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Isotropic stable kernels.}
Proposition~\ref{prop:stable-kernel-input-package} gives, with
\(\beta=d+\alpha\),
\[
 p(y)\asymp(1+|y|)^{-\beta},\qquad
 |\partial^\gamma p(y)|\le C_\gamma
 (1+|y|)^{-\beta-|\gamma|}.
\]
This proves \textup{(K1)}.  For \(|h|\le1\), the mean-value theorem, the
bound at order \(r+1\), and
\(1+|y|\le2(1+|y-th|)\) give
\[
 \frac{|\partial^\gamma p(y-h)-\partial^\gamma p(y)|}{p(y)}
 \le C|h|,\qquad |\gamma|=r,\quad 0\le t\le1,
\]
so \textup{(K2)} holds.  The two-sided density estimate also gives
\[
 \mathsf H_p(z)\le C+(d+\alpha)\log(1+|z|),
\]
which is \textup{(K3)} for every \(r\ge1\).  The abstract definitions of
\(F_y^p\), \(B_{p,j}^\lambda\), \(A_{p,s}^\lambda\), and
\(\mathcal Q_{p,r}\) are respectively the definitions used in
Theorem~\ref{thm:stable-arbitrary-order-law-by-law-decomposition}.  Hence
that theorem is precisely this specialization, not a weakened consequence.

\emph{Poisson kernel.}
At unit height the Poisson kernel on \(\RR^d\) is
\[
 P_1^{(d)}(y)=c_d(1+|y|^2)^{-(d+1)/2}.
\]
The derivative calculation in the next paragraph with \(\beta=d+1\)
verifies \textup{(K1)} and \textup{(K2)}, while the displayed entropy bound
there verifies \textup{(K3)}.  Its spatial dilates are exactly
\(P_s^{(d)}(x)=c_ds(s^2+|x|^2)^{-(d+1)/2}\).  This is also the
\(\alpha=1\) stable member, but the rational formula proves membership
directly, without stable-density estimates.

\emph{Multivariate Student kernels.}
Let \(\beta>d\).  Repeated differentiation shows that every normalized
derivative \(\partial^\gamma p_{\beta,d}/p_{\beta,d}\) is a finite sum of
terms
\[
 c_{\gamma,k}\,
 \frac{y^{\delta_{\gamma,k}}}{(1+|y|^2)^{k}},
 \qquad |\delta_{\gamma,k}|\le2k,
\]
and is therefore bounded.  The same statement one order higher, together
with
\[
 \frac{p_{\beta,d}(y-th)}{p_{\beta,d}(y)}\le C_{\beta,d},
 \qquad |h|\le1,\quad 0\le t\le1,
\]
and the mean-value theorem gives \(\omega_{p,r}(t)\le Ct\).  Finally,
\[
 \sqrt{1+|w+z|^2}
 \le\sqrt{1+|w|^2}+|z|
 \le(1+|z|)\sqrt{1+|w|^2},
\]
so
\[
 \log\frac{p_{\beta,d}(w)}{p_{\beta,d}(w+z)}
 \le\beta\log(1+|z|).
\]
Integration against \(p_{\beta,d}(w)\dd w\) proves \textup{(K3)}.
This supplies a nonstable polynomial-tail family for every \(\beta>d\);
only the value \(\beta=d+1\) is the Poisson/stable case.

\emph{Gaussian obstruction.}
For the standard Gaussian \(g\),
\[
 \partial^\gamma g/g=(-1)^{|\gamma|}H_\gamma,
 \qquad \nabla\log g(y)=-y,
\]
so the normalized derivatives are unbounded and \textup{(K1)} fails at its
first derivative.  The translate quotient
\[
 F_y^g(z)=\exp\{y\mathbin\cdot z-|z|^2/2\}
\]
has infimum zero for every \(z\ne0\); hence the uniform lower bound for the
actual quotients used by the two-background expansion is unavailable.  The
proxy failure is already explicit in dimension one at \(r=2\).  Take
\(\nu=\delta_m\) and
\(\eta=(\delta_{m-a}+\delta_{m+a})/2\), where \(m\ne0\) and \(a>0\).
They have the same moments below order two.  For \(s>|m|+a\) the retained
tails vanish, while the common first Gaussian mode is \(my\); therefore both
prescribed proxies equal \(1+my/s\), which is negative on a half-line.
Thus proxy positivity, the background lower bound, and the bounded cubic
remainder all fail at the same additive-jet step.  Gaussian translate entropy
itself is not the obstruction when \(r\ge2\), since
\(\mathsf H_g(z)=|z|^2/2\).  Weighted Hermite arguments can and do prove
Gaussian first-unmatched-moment asymptotics under stronger input hypotheses,
as recalled above, but they are not instances of this positive additive
tail-proxy mechanism.
\end{proof}


 succeeded in 209ms:
\begin{corollary}[A sufficient moment condition for defect vanishing]
\label{cor:arbitrary-tail-defect-positive-moment-condition}
Under the hypotheses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:stable-arbitrary-order-law-by-law-decomposition}, suppose in
addition that \(\Delta_r\ne0\) and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-positive-moment-condition}
 \int_{\RR^d}|x|^{p_{r,\alpha,d}}(\nu+\eta)(\dd x)<\infty.
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-positive-limit}
 s^{2r}\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)\longrightarrow0.
\end{equation}
This condition includes noncompact pairs of laws.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:all-order-stable-first-unmatched-moment} and
\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-decomposition} give, respectively,
\begin{align*}
 D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
 &=\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)s^{-2r}+o(s^{-2r}),\\
 D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
 &=\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)s^{-2r}
   +\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)+o(s^{-2r}).
\end{align*}
Subtracting proves \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-positive-limit}.

For a concrete noncompact class, use the finite-support probability blocks
from Lemma~\ref{lem:stable-finite-difference-blocks}.  Convolve \(A_R^+\) and
\(A_R^-\) with the same nondegenerate Gaussian law.  The multinomial formula
shows that the convolved laws still match below order \(r\), while their
order-\(r\) difference is the nonzero difference of the two blocks: every
other term contains a block-moment difference of order below \(r\).  Both
convolved laws are noncompact and have moments of every order, so
\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-positive-moment-condition} applies.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Non-vacuousness of the tail-defect criterion]
\label{cor:arbitrary-tail-defect-nonvacuousness}
If \(d+\alpha>2r\), then for every
\(r\le\rho<p_{r,\alpha,d}\) there are probability laws \(\nu,\eta\) with
finite \(\rho\)-th absolute moments, matching every tensor moment below
order \(r\), and having a finite nonzero order-\(r\) tensor difference, for
which
\begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-unbounded-limsup}
 \limsup_{s\to\infty}s^{2r}\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)=+\infty.
\end{equation}
In particular, vanishing of the explicit nonnegative tail-defect energy is
not automatic under the minimal finite \(r\)-th moment hypothesis.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Use the laws in the sharpness assertion
\eqref{eq:all-order-stable-sharpness}.  They satisfy the hypotheses of the
law-by-law decomposition because \(\rho\ge r\).  Multiplying
\eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-decomposition} by \(s^{2r}\) gives
\[
 s^{2r}D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
 =\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)
  +s^{2r}\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)+o(1).
\]
The quadratic term is finite and independent of \(s\), whereas the left
side has infinite limsup by \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-sharpness}.  This
forces \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-unbounded-limsup}.
\end{proof}

The theorem is a specialization of the unnumbered abstract kernel theorem
that precedes it.  For a strictly positive density \(p\), the proof uses only
bounded normalized derivatives through order \(r\), uniform
translation-continuity of the normalized order-\(r\) derivative, and an
order-\(r\) bound on the entropy of a translate of \(p\).  These conditions
hold for all isotropic strictly stable densities, for the Poisson kernel, and
for every multivariate Student density \(c_{\beta,d}(1+|y|^2)^{-\beta/2}\),
\(\beta>d\).  No tail asymptotic or regular variation enters the abstract
proof.  The Gaussian density does not satisfy the bounded normalized-jet
condition: its normalized derivatives are Hermite polynomials, and an
additive common jet need not remain positive.  Thus the Gaussian
first-unmatched-moment theorem recalled above uses a different weighted
Hermite mechanism; it is not presented as a member of the positive
tail-proxy theorem.

We claim neither that this proxy is unique nor that it is canonical among
possible positive proxy constructions.  Fixed-scale robustness concerns the
explicit, non-vacuous convention class just described, not every reasonable
proxy normalization.  The second result is asymptotic coefficient rigidity
inside a prescribed order-\(r\) jet-faithful ansatz; it does not assert
blanket canonicity, uniqueness, or entropy minimality.
