 succeeded in 244ms:
   1: Integrating over the ball and summing against all probability weights of
   2: \(\eta\) yields
   3: \[
   4:  (p_{s_j}^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)(E_j)
   5:  \le C_{\alpha,d,r}(s_j/R_j)^\beta
   6:  =C_{\alpha,d,r}\kappa^\beta w_j,
   7: \]
   8: which is the second assertion.
   9: \end{proof}
  10: 
  11: \begin{lemma}[Uniform Bernoulli relative-entropy separation]
  12: \label{lem:bernoulli-kl-linear-separation}
  13: Let \(0<b<a\).  If \(u\ge aw\) and \(v\le bw\) are Bernoulli parameters,
  14: then
  15: \begin{equation}\label{eq:bernoulli-kl-linear-separation}
  16:  D_{\rm KL}(\operatorname{Ber}(u)\|\operatorname{Ber}(v))
  17:  \ge a\left\{\log\frac ab-1+\frac ba\right\}w.
  18: \end{equation}
  19: \end{lemma}
  20: 
  21: \begin{proof}
  22: If \(v=0<u\), the relative entropy is infinite.  Otherwise \(u>v>0\), and
  23: \[
  24:  (1-u)\log\frac{1-u}{1-v}\ge-(u-v).
  25: \]
  26: Writing \(\theta=v/u\le b/a<1\) therefore gives
  27: \[
  28:  D_{\rm KL}(\operatorname{Ber}(u)\|\operatorname{Ber}(v))
  29:  \ge u\{\log(1/\theta)-1+\theta\}
  30:  \ge aw\left\{\log\frac ab-1+\frac ba\right\},
  31: \]
  32: because the braced function is decreasing on \((0,1)\).
  33: \end{proof}
  34: 
  35: \paragraph{Gaussian benchmark and priority.}
  36: Let \(\rho_t(x)=(2\pi t)^{-d/2}e^{-|x|^2/(2t)}\).  Under a finite
  37: exponential square moment for each centred input, Chen and Niles-Weed
  38: \cite[Theorem~2.5]{ChenNilesWeed2022SmoothedWasserstein} prove that moment
  39: matching below order \(r=n+1\), followed by a nonzero difference at order
  40: \(r\), implies, with
  41: \(\Delta m_\gamma=m_\gamma(\mu)-m_\gamma(\eta)\),
  42: \begin{equation}\label{eq:chen-niles-weed-gaussian-kl-benchmark}
  43:  \lim_{t\to\infty}t^rD_{\rm KL}(\mu*\rho_t\|\eta*\rho_t)
  44:  =\frac12\sum_{|\gamma|=r}\frac{|\Delta m_\gamma|^2}{\gamma!}.
  45: \end{equation}
  46: Putting \(s=\sqrt t\) gives the same \(s^{-2r}\) scaling as below.  For the
  47: standard Gaussian \(g=\rho_1\), the identities
  48: \((-1)^{|\gamma|}\partial^\gamma g=H_\gamma g\) and
  49: \(\int H_\gamma H_\delta g=\gamma!\mathbf1_{\gamma=\delta}\) identify the
  50: right side of \eqref{eq:chen-niles-weed-gaussian-kl-benchmark} exactly with
  51: the quadratic functional \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-leading-functional} at
  52: the Gaussian boundary \(\alpha=2\).  Accordingly, the first-unmatched
  53: quadratic KL mechanism and its positivity are prior work in the Gaussian
  54: case.  The theorem below contributes the heavy-tailed range \(0<\alpha<2\),
  55: the critical complete-moment exponent, endpoint sufficiency, and uniform
  56: sharpness over those moment classes.
  57: 
  58: \begin{theorem}[All-order first unmatched tensor under stable smoothing]
  59: \label{thm:all-order-stable-first-unmatched-moment}
  60: Let \(d\ge1\), \(0<\alpha<2\), and let \(r\ge1\) be an integer.  Define
  61: \(q_{r,\alpha,d}\) and \(p_{r,\alpha,d}\) by
  62: \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-critical-exponents}.  Let \(\nu,\eta\) be
  63: probability laws on \(\RR^d\) such that
  64: \begin{equation}\label{eq:all-order-stable-moment-hypotheses}
  65:  m_\gamma(\nu)=m_\gamma(\eta)\quad(|\gamma|<r),
  66:  \qquad
  67:  \int|x|^{p_{r,\alpha,d}}(\nu+\eta)(\dd x)<\infty.
  68: \end{equation}
  69: Put
  70: \[
  71:  \Delta_r:=\int_{\RR^d}x^{\otimes r}(\nu-\eta)(\dd x)
  72:  \in\operatorname{Sym}^r(\RR^d),
  73: \]
  74: so its multi-index coordinates are
  75: \(\Delta m_\gamma=m_\gamma(\nu)-m_\gamma(\eta)\), and suppose
  76: \(\Delta_r\ne0\).  With the Euclidean contraction of symmetric tensors,
  77: define
  78: \begin{equation}\label{eq:all-order-stable-leading-mode}
  79:  B_{\Delta_r}(y):=
  80:  \frac{(-1)^r}{r!p_1^{(\alpha,d)}(y)}
  81:  \langle\Delta_r,D^rp_1^{(\alpha,d)}(y)\rangle
  82:  =\frac{(-1)^r}{p_1^{(\alpha,d)}(y)}
  83:  \sum_{|\gamma|=r}\frac{\Delta m_\gamma}{\gamma!}
  84:  \partial^\gamma p_1^{(\alpha,d)}(y)
  85: \end{equation}
  86: and
  87: \begin{equation}\label{eq:all-order-stable-leading-functional}
  88:  \mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)
  89:  :=\frac12\int_{\RR^d}B_{\Delta_r}(y)^2
  90:                   \Omega_{\alpha,d}(\dd y).
  91: \end{equation}
  92: Then \(\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)>0\), the quadratic form is
  93: invariant under \(\Delta_r\mapsto U^{\otimes r}\Delta_r\) for every
  94: \(U\in O(d)\), and
  95: \begin{equation}\label{eq:all-order-stable-first-unmatched-asymptotic}
  96:  D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
  97:  =\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)s^{-2r}+o(s^{-2r}).
  98: \end{equation}
  99: 
 100: The exponent \(p_{r,\alpha,d}\) is optimal as a uniform sufficient
 101: absolute-moment hypothesis, with its endpoint included.  More precisely,
 102: if \(d+\alpha>2r\), then for every
 103: \(r\le\rho<p_{r,\alpha,d}\) there are probability laws \(\nu,\eta\)
 104: having finite \(\rho\)th absolute moments, matching every tensor moment
 105: below order \(r\), and having a finite nonzero order-\(r\) tensor difference,
 106: but
 107: \begin{equation}\label{eq:all-order-stable-sharpness}
 108:  \limsup_{s\to\infty}s^{2r}
 109:  D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)=+\infty.
 110: \end{equation}
 111: For \(0<\rho<r\), finiteness of the \(\rho\)th absolute moment does not
 112: even guarantee that the tensor in \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-leading-mode}
 113: is defined throughout the moment class.  Thus the latter observation is a
 114: domain obstruction, whereas \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-sharpness} is the
 115: analytic sharpness statement on laws for which the leading tensor exists.
 116: \end{theorem}
 117: 
 118: \begin{proof}
 119: Write \(q=q_{r,\alpha,d}\), \(p=p_{r,\alpha,d}\), and
 120: \(m=\lfloor p\rfloor\).  Apply
 121: Lemma~\ref{lem:all-order-stable-critical-remainder} to both laws.  Their
 122: common moments below order \(r\) cancel, so
 123: \begin{equation}\label{eq:all-order-stable-two-quotient-split}
 124:  q_s^\nu-q_s^\eta=v_s+e_s,
 125:  \quad
 126:  v_s=\sum_{j=r}^m s^{-j}(B_j^\nu-B_j^\eta),
 127:  \quad
 128:  e_s=r_s^\nu-r_s^\eta.
 129: \end{equation}
 130: In particular,
 131: \[
 132:  s^rv_s\longrightarrow B_{\Delta_r}\quad\hbox{in }L^\infty,
 133:  \qquad \|e_s\|_q=o(s^{-p}).
 134: \]
 135: The intermediate modes \(r<j\le m\) in
 136: \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-two-quotient-split} are essential: unless their
 137: moments also match, they are not part of the \(o(s^{-p})\) remainder.
 138: 
 139: No part of this argument requires \(r\ge2\).  At \(r=1\), the endpoint
 140: formula still gives \(p\ge1\), hence the assumed \(p\)-moment supplies the
 141: first moments and \(\EE|X|<\infty\); also \(m+1>p\) and \(pq=2\).  Thus the
 142: critical remainder and the two-background transfer have exactly the powers
 143: needed for an \(o(s^{-2})\) error.  Lemma~\ref{lem:stable-finite-difference-blocks}
 144: records the corresponding first-difference block used for sharpness.
 145: 
 146: Proposition~\ref{prop:stable-kernel-input-package} implies
 147: \(\|\nabla\log p_1^{(\alpha,d)}\|_\infty\le L_{\alpha,d}\).  Hence, if
 148: \(X\) has law \(\lambda\),
 149: \[
 150:  q_s^\lambda(y)
 151:  \ge\EE\exp(-L_{\alpha,d}|X|/s)
 152:  \ge\exp(-L_{\alpha,d}\EE|X|/s),
 153: \]
 154: uniformly in \(y\).  The last expression tends to one.  Translation
 155: continuity of \(p_1^{(\alpha,d)}\) in \(L^1\) gives, by Tonelli and dominated
 156: convergence,
 157: \[
 158:  \|q_s^\eta-1\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}
 159:  \le\int\|p_1^{(\alpha,d)}(\mathord\cdot-x/s)
 160:               -p_1^{(\alpha,d)}\|_1\,\eta(\dd x)\longrightarrow0;
 161: \]
 162: the integrand is bounded by two.  A second Tonelli calculation gives
 163: \(\int q_s^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=1\) for every probability law
 164: \(\lambda\).  These are precisely the positive lower bound, mass
 165: normalization, and \(L^1\)-convergence required of the denominator by
 166: Lemma~\ref{lem:two-background-critical-bregman-transfer}; no
 167: \(L^\infty\)-convergence of \(q_s^\eta\) to one is used.  Taking
 168: \(g_s=q_s^\eta\) in that lemma proves
 169: \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-first-unmatched-asymptotic}.
 170: 
 171: Boundedness of \(B_{\Delta_r}\) makes
 172: \(\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}\) finite.  If it were zero, then
 173: \[
 174:  \sum_{|\gamma|=r}\frac{\Delta m_\gamma}{\gamma!}
 175:  \partial^\gamma p_1^{(\alpha,d)}=0
 176: \]
 177: almost everywhere.  Fourier transformation in the sense of tempered
 178: distributions gives a homogeneous polynomial in \(\xi\), with coefficients
 179: \(\Delta m_\gamma/\gamma!\), multiplied by the nowhere-vanishing stable
 180: characteristic function.  The polynomial must vanish identically, contrary
 181: to \(\Delta_r\ne0\).  This proves strict positivity.  If \(U\in O(d)\),
 182: isotropy and the tensor chain rule give
 183: \(B_{U^{\otimes r}\Delta_r}(Uy)=B_{\Delta_r}(y)\).  Orthogonal invariance of
 184: \(\Omega_{\alpha,d}\) then proves the asserted invariance of \(\mathcal Q\).
 185: For \(d\ge2\), under the homogeneous-polynomial identification, the standard
 186: trace/harmonic decomposition
 187: \[
 188:  \operatorname{Sym}^r(\RR^d)
 189:  =\bigoplus_{k=0}^{\lfloor r/2\rfloor}
 190:    |x|^{2k}\mathcal H_{r-2k}
 191: \]
 192: is multiplicity-free under \(O(d)\).  Hence the self-adjoint operator of
 193: \(\mathcal Q\) is scalar on each harmonic summand; the positivity just proved
 194: shows that all these scalars are strictly positive.  No formula for the
 195: individual scalars is needed below.
 196: 
 197: It remains to establish sharpness.  Assume \(\beta=d+\alpha>2r\), so
 198: \[
 199:  q=1+\frac{2r}{\beta},\qquad p=\frac{2r}{q}>r.
 200: \]
 201: Fix a unit vector \(e\), and let \(A_R^\pm\) be the probability blocks from
 202: Lemma~\ref{lem:stable-finite-difference-blocks}.
 203: 
 204: Choose \(a_j>0\) with \(\sum_ja_j<\infty\).  Choose the radii recursively so
 205: that
 206: \begin{equation}\label{eq:all-order-separated-block-radii}
 207:  R_{j+1}>4(r+1)R_j,
 208: \end{equation}
 209: and enlarge each new radius further whenever necessary below.  Put
 210: \(w_j=a_jR_j^{-\rho}\), with \(\sum_jw_j<1\), and define
 211: \[
 212:  \nu=\left(1-\sum_jw_j\right)\delta_0+\sum_jw_jA_{R_j}^+,
 213:  \qquad
 214:  \eta=\left(1-\sum_jw_j\right)\delta_0+\sum_jw_jA_{R_j}^-.
 215: \]
 216: They have finite \(\rho\)th moments.  Since \(\rho\ge r\),
 217: \eqref{eq:stable-block-moment-identities} also shows that their
 218: order-\(r\) tensor difference is finite and nonzero.
 219: 
 220: The endpoint \(x_j=(r+1)R_je\) belongs to \(A_{R_j}^+\) and not to
 221: \(A_{R_j}^-\).  Let \(E_j\) be the ball of radius
 222: \[
 223:  s_j=\kappa R_jw_j^{1/\beta}
 224: \]
 225: about \(x_j\).  Recursive enlargement of \(R_j\) makes \(s_j\to\infty\);
 226: also \(s_j/R_j=\kappa w_j^{1/\beta}<1/4\) for all sufficiently large \(j\).
 227: Lemma~\ref{lem:stable-moving-ball-probability-separation} gives both
 228: probability bounds, after explicitly separating \(E_j\) from every
 229: opposite-sign atom in the same, preceding, and succeeding blocks.
 230: Choose \(\kappa\) so that
 500: \begin{remark}[What the decomposition resolves]
 501: When \(d+\alpha\le4\), one has \(p_{\alpha,d}=2\), so every
 502: finite-covariance law makes the tail energy \(o(s^{-4})\) by
 503: Theorem~\ref{thm:high-dimensional-kl-moment-threshold}.  When
 504: \(d+\alpha>4\), finite covariance alone need not do so.  In that regime
 505: \eqref{eq:stable-law-by-law-criterion} is the exact missing law-by-law
 506: condition: it neither replaces the nonlinear tail energy by a moment nor
 507: assumes regular variation.
 508: \end{remark}
 509: 
 510: The finite-covariance proof above uses positivity of the one-sided raw-tail
 511: potential.  At higher order the natural object is a pair of laws, and the
 512: difference of their raw-tail potentials is signed.  Applying \(\Phi\) to that
 513: difference would therefore neither be well-defined in general nor preserve
 514: nonnegativity.  The following relative \(\Phi\)-energy is the replacement.
 515: 
 516: \input{sec_two_background_kl_lemma}
 517: 
 518: \input{sec_abstract_kernel_tail_decomposition}
 519: 
 520: \begin{theorem}[Arbitrary-order law-by-law tail-defect decomposition]
 521: \label{thm:stable-arbitrary-order-law-by-law-decomposition}
 522: Let \(d\ge1\), \(0<\alpha<2\), and let \(r\ge1\) be an integer.  Let
 523: \(\nu,\eta\) be probability laws on \(\RR^d\) satisfying
 524: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-moment-hypotheses}
 525:  m_\gamma(\nu)=m_\gamma(\eta)\quad(|\gamma|<r),
 526:  \qquad
 527:  \int_{\RR^d}|x|^r(\nu+\eta)(\dd x)<\infty.
 528: \end{equation}
 529: For \(\lambda\in\{\nu,\eta\}\), put
 530: \[
 531:  \tau_s^\lambda=\lambda(|x|>s),\qquad
 532:  V_s^\lambda(y)=\int_{\{|x|>s\}}
 533:  F_y^{(\alpha,d)}(x/s)\lambda(\dd x),
 534: \]
 535: and use the moment modes \(B_j^\lambda\) from
 536: \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-moment-modes}.  Their values agree for \(j<r\);
 537: write
 538: \[
 539:  C_{r-1,s}(y)=\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}s^{-j}B_j^\nu(y)
 540:              =\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}s^{-j}B_j^\eta(y),
 541: \]
 542: with an empty sum when \(r=1\), and define
 543: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-proxy-quotients}
 544:  A_s^\lambda(y)=1+C_{r-1,s}(y)+V_s^\lambda(y)-\tau_s^\lambda.
 545: \end{equation}
 546: For all sufficiently large \(s\), both \(A_s^\lambda\) are positive,
 547: integrate to one against \(\Omega_{\alpha,d}\), and hence define probability
 548: densities
 549: \[
 550:  k_{r,s}^\lambda(x)=s^{-d}p_1^{(\alpha,d)}(x/s)
 551:                     A_s^\lambda(x/s).
 552: \]
 553:  The explicit nonnegative tail-defect energy
 554: \begin{align}
 555:  \mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)
 556:  &:=D_{\rm KL}(k_{r,s}^\nu\|k_{r,s}^\eta)\notag\\
 557:  &=\int_{\RR^d}A_s^\eta
 558:  \Phi\!\left(\frac{A_s^\nu-A_s^\eta}{A_s^\eta}\right)
 559:  \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
 560:  \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-energy}
 561: \end{align}
 562: is finite, and, with
 563: \(\Delta_r=\int x^{\otimes r}(\nu-\eta)(\dd x)\), one has
 564: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-decomposition}
 565:  D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
 566:  =\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)s^{-2r}
 567:   +\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)+o(s^{-2r}).
 568: \end{equation}
 569: In particular, law by law,
 570: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-criterion}
 571:  s^{2r}D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
 572:  \longrightarrow\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)
 573:  \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 574:  s^{2r}\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)\longrightarrow0.
 575: \end{equation}
 576: No regular-variation or absolute-continuity hypothesis, and no moment above
 577: order \(r\), is required.
 578: \end{theorem}
 579: 
 580: \begin{proof}
 581: Write \(p_1=p_1^{(\alpha,d)}\), \(F_y=F_y^{(\alpha,d)}\), and let
 582: \(\mathsf B_j(y,z)\) be the homogeneous degree-\(j\) Taylor term of
 583: \(F_y(z)\) at zero.  The stable-kernel estimates in
 584: Proposition~\ref{prop:stable-kernel-input-package}, Taylor's formula, and
 585: translation continuity of \(D^rp_1\) in \(L^1\) give, for \(|z|\le1\),
 586: \begin{align}
 587:  &\left\|F_\cdot(z)-\sum_{j=0}^r\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\right\|_\infty
 588:  +\left\|F_\cdot(z)-\sum_{j=0}^r\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)
 589:   \right\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}
 590:  \le\varepsilon_r(|z|)|z|^r,
 591:  \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-local-remainder}\\
 592:  &\|\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\|_\infty
 593:  +\|\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}
 594:  \le C_r|z|^j,\qquad 1\le j\le r,
 595:  \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-mode-bounds}
 596: \end{align}
 597: where \(\varepsilon_r\) is bounded on \([0,1]\) and tends to zero at
 598: zero.  For the uniform part one may use the bounded order-\((r+1)\)
 599: derivative quotient, giving the stronger \(O(|z|^{r+1})\) bound.  For the
 600: \(L^1\) part, multiplication by \(p_1(y)\) reduces the integral remainder
 601: to translates of \(D^rp_1\), exactly as in
 602: \eqref{eq:stable-local-taylor-modulus}.
 603: 
 604: Set
 605: \[
 606:  q_s^\lambda(y)=\int F_y(x/s)\lambda(\dd x).
 607: \]
 608: Splitting the integral at \(|x|=s\), retaining the raw exterior translate,
 609: and using the moment identities gives the exact decomposition
 610: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-exact-quotient-split}
 611:  q_s^\lambda=A_s^\lambda+s^{-r}B_r^\lambda+R_s^\lambda.
 612: \end{equation}
 613: Here \(R_s^\lambda\) is the interior Taylor remainder through order \(r\)
 614: minus the exterior Taylor modes of orders \(1,\ldots,r\).  Equations
 615: \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-local-remainder} and
 616: \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-mode-bounds} imply
 617: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-remainder-smallness}
 618:  \|R_s^\lambda\|_\infty+
 619:  \|R_s^\lambda\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}=o(s^{-r}).
 620: \end{equation}
 621: Indeed, after multiplication by \(s^r\), the interior term is dominated by
 622: \(C|x|^r\) and converges pointwise to zero, while for \(j\le r\),
 623: \[
 624:  s^{-j}\int_{|x|>s}|x|^j\lambda(\dd x)
 625:  \le s^{-r}\int_{|x|>s}|x|^r\lambda(\dd x)=o(s^{-r}).
 626: \]
 627: The same last estimate gives
 628: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-mass-smallness}
 629:  \tau_s^\lambda=o(s^{-r}).
 630: \end{equation}
 631: 
 632: Translation preserves mass, so
 633: \[
 634:  V_s^\lambda\ge0,\qquad
 635:  \int V_s^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=\tau_s^\lambda.
 636: \]
 637: Also \(\int B_j^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=0\), by integration of the
 638: derivatives in \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-moment-modes}.  Therefore
 639: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-proxy-normalization}
 640:  \int A_s^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=1.
 641: \end{equation}
 642: Moreover,
 643: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-proxy-positive-lower-bound}
 644:  \inf_yA_s^\lambda(y)
 645:  \ge1-\|C_{r-1,s}\|_\infty-\tau_s^\lambda\longrightarrow1,
 646: \end{equation}
 647: because \(V_s^\lambda\ge0\),
 648: \(\|C_{r-1,s}\|_\infty=O(s^{-1})\) (with value zero when \(r=1\)), and
 649: \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-mass-smallness} holds.  This proves positivity,
 650: including at \(r=1\), and gives a common lower bound such as \(1/2\).
 651: 
 652: We next prove proxy-entropy finiteness without appealing to the covariance
 653: case.  The two-sided stable-density bound in
 654: Proposition~\ref{prop:stable-kernel-input-package} and
 655: \(1+|w+z|\le(1+|w|)(1+|z|)\) give
 656: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-stable-translate-entropy}
 657:  D_{\rm KL}\!\left(p_1(\mathord\cdot-z)\,\middle\|\,p_1\right)
 658:  \le C_{\alpha,d}+(d+\alpha)\log(1+|z|).
 659: \end{equation}
 660: If \(\tau_s^\lambda>0\), write \(V_s^\lambda=\tau_s^\lambda Q_s^\lambda\),
 661: where \(Q_s^\lambda\) is the quotient, relative to \(p_1\), of the
 662: normalized exterior translate mixture.  Convexity of relative entropy and
 663: \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-stable-translate-entropy} imply
 664: \begin{align}
 665:  \int Q_s^\lambda\log Q_s^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
 666:  &\le\frac1{\tau_s^\lambda}\int_{|x|>s}
 667:  \left\{C_{\alpha,d}+(d+\alpha)\log(1+|x|/s)\right\}
 668:  \lambda(\dd x)\notag\\
 669:  &<\infty,
 670:  \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-normalized-proxy-entropy}
 671: \end{align}
 672: since \(\log(1+|x|/s)\le |x|/s\) and the assumed \(r\)-th moment, with
 673: \(r\ge1\), supplies a finite first moment.  For \(0\le\tau\le1\),
 674: \(\Phi(\tau q)\le\tau q\log(1+q)\); hence
 675: \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-normalized-proxy-entropy}, together with the bound
 676: of the negative part of \(q\log q\), proves
 677: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-potential-entropy-finite}
 678:  \int\Phi(V_s^\lambda)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}<\infty.
 679: \end{equation}
 680: The conclusion is immediate when \(\tau_s^\lambda=0\).
 681: 
 682: For large \(s\), \(|C_{r-1,s}-\tau_s^\lambda|\le1/2\), so
 683: \(A_s^\lambda\le3/2+V_s^\lambda\).  The elementary bound
 684: \[
 685:  (u+v)\log^+(u+v)\le C\{1+v+\Phi(v)\},
 686:  \qquad 0\le u\le3/2,\quad v\ge0,
 687: \]
 688: and \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-potential-entropy-finite} show that
 689: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-proxy-positive-entropy-finite}
 690:  \int A_s^\lambda\log^+A_s^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}<\infty.
 691: \end{equation}
 692: Finally \(A_s^\eta\ge1/2\).  The positive part of
 693: \(A_s^\nu\log(A_s^\nu/A_s^\eta)\) is bounded by
 694: \(A_s^\nu\log^+(2A_s^\nu)\), while its negative part is bounded in absolute
 695: integral by \(e^{-1}\int A_s^\eta\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=e^{-1}\).
 696: Thus \(\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)<\infty\), as asserted.
 697: 
 698: It remains to verify, rather than assume, the hypotheses of the abstract
 699: two-background KL perturbation lemma.  Put
 700: \(\epsilon_s=s^{-r}\), take

 500: \begin{remark}[What the decomposition resolves]
 501: When \(d+\alpha\le4\), one has \(p_{\alpha,d}=2\), so every
 502: finite-covariance law makes the tail energy \(o(s^{-4})\) by
 503: Theorem~\ref{thm:high-dimensional-kl-moment-threshold}.  When
 504: \(d+\alpha>4\), finite covariance alone need not do so.  In that regime
 505: \eqref{eq:stable-law-by-law-criterion} is the exact missing law-by-law
 506: condition: it neither replaces the nonlinear tail energy by a moment nor
 507: assumes regular variation.
 508: \end{remark}
 509: 
 510: The finite-covariance proof above uses positivity of the one-sided raw-tail
 511: potential.  At higher order the natural object is a pair of laws, and the
 512: difference of their raw-tail potentials is signed.  Applying \(\Phi\) to that
 513: difference would therefore neither be well-defined in general nor preserve
 514: nonnegativity.  The following relative \(\Phi\)-energy is the replacement.
 515: 
 516: \input{sec_two_background_kl_lemma}
 517: 
 518: \input{sec_abstract_kernel_tail_decomposition}
 519: 
 520: \begin{theorem}[Arbitrary-order law-by-law tail-defect decomposition]
 521: \label{thm:stable-arbitrary-order-law-by-law-decomposition}
 522: Let \(d\ge1\), \(0<\alpha<2\), and let \(r\ge1\) be an integer.  Let
 523: \(\nu,\eta\) be probability laws on \(\RR^d\) satisfying
 524: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-moment-hypotheses}
 525:  m_\gamma(\nu)=m_\gamma(\eta)\quad(|\gamma|<r),
 526:  \qquad
 527:  \int_{\RR^d}|x|^r(\nu+\eta)(\dd x)<\infty.
 528: \end{equation}
 529: For \(\lambda\in\{\nu,\eta\}\), put
 530: \[
 531:  \tau_s^\lambda=\lambda(|x|>s),\qquad
 532:  V_s^\lambda(y)=\int_{\{|x|>s\}}
 533:  F_y^{(\alpha,d)}(x/s)\lambda(\dd x),
 534: \]
 535: and use the moment modes \(B_j^\lambda\) from
 536: \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-moment-modes}.  Their values agree for \(j<r\);
 537: write
 538: \[
 539:  C_{r-1,s}(y)=\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}s^{-j}B_j^\nu(y)
 540:              =\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}s^{-j}B_j^\eta(y),
 541: \]
 542: with an empty sum when \(r=1\), and define
 543: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-proxy-quotients}
 544:  A_s^\lambda(y)=1+C_{r-1,s}(y)+V_s^\lambda(y)-\tau_s^\lambda.
 545: \end{equation}
 546: For all sufficiently large \(s\), both \(A_s^\lambda\) are positive,
 547: integrate to one against \(\Omega_{\alpha,d}\), and hence define probability
 548: densities
 549: \[
 550:  k_{r,s}^\lambda(x)=s^{-d}p_1^{(\alpha,d)}(x/s)
 551:                     A_s^\lambda(x/s).
 552: \]
 553:  The explicit nonnegative tail-defect energy
 554: \begin{align}
 555:  \mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)
 556:  &:=D_{\rm KL}(k_{r,s}^\nu\|k_{r,s}^\eta)\notag\\
 557:  &=\int_{\RR^d}A_s^\eta
 558:  \Phi\!\left(\frac{A_s^\nu-A_s^\eta}{A_s^\eta}\right)
 559:  \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
 560:  \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-energy}
 561: \end{align}
 562: is finite, and, with
 563: \(\Delta_r=\int x^{\otimes r}(\nu-\eta)(\dd x)\), one has
 564: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-decomposition}
 565:  D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
 566:  =\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)s^{-2r}
 567:   +\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)+o(s^{-2r}).
 568: \end{equation}
 569: In particular, law by law,
 570: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-criterion}
 571:  s^{2r}D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
 572:  \longrightarrow\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)
 573:  \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 574:  s^{2r}\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)\longrightarrow0.
 575: \end{equation}
 576: No regular-variation or absolute-continuity hypothesis, and no moment above
 577: order \(r\), is required.
 578: \end{theorem}
 579: 
 580: \begin{proof}
 581: Write \(p_1=p_1^{(\alpha,d)}\), \(F_y=F_y^{(\alpha,d)}\), and let
 582: \(\mathsf B_j(y,z)\) be the homogeneous degree-\(j\) Taylor term of
 583: \(F_y(z)\) at zero.  The stable-kernel estimates in
 584: Proposition~\ref{prop:stable-kernel-input-package}, Taylor's formula, and
 585: translation continuity of \(D^rp_1\) in \(L^1\) give, for \(|z|\le1\),
 586: \begin{align}
 587:  &\left\|F_\cdot(z)-\sum_{j=0}^r\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\right\|_\infty
 588:  +\left\|F_\cdot(z)-\sum_{j=0}^r\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)
 589:   \right\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}
 590:  \le\varepsilon_r(|z|)|z|^r,
 591:  \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-local-remainder}\\
 592:  &\|\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\|_\infty
 593:  +\|\mathsf B_j(\cdot,z)\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}
 594:  \le C_r|z|^j,\qquad 1\le j\le r,
 595:  \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-mode-bounds}
 596: \end{align}
 597: where \(\varepsilon_r\) is bounded on \([0,1]\) and tends to zero at
 598: zero.  For the uniform part one may use the bounded order-\((r+1)\)
 599: derivative quotient, giving the stronger \(O(|z|^{r+1})\) bound.  For the
 600: \(L^1\) part, multiplication by \(p_1(y)\) reduces the integral remainder
 601: to translates of \(D^rp_1\), exactly as in
 602: \eqref{eq:stable-local-taylor-modulus}.
 603: 
 604: Set
 605: \[
 606:  q_s^\lambda(y)=\int F_y(x/s)\lambda(\dd x).
 607: \]
 608: Splitting the integral at \(|x|=s\), retaining the raw exterior translate,
 609: and using the moment identities gives the exact decomposition
 610: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-exact-quotient-split}
 611:  q_s^\lambda=A_s^\lambda+s^{-r}B_r^\lambda+R_s^\lambda.
 612: \end{equation}
 613: Here \(R_s^\lambda\) is the interior Taylor remainder through order \(r\)
 614: minus the exterior Taylor modes of orders \(1,\ldots,r\).  Equations
 615: \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-local-remainder} and
 616: \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-mode-bounds} imply
 617: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-remainder-smallness}
 618:  \|R_s^\lambda\|_\infty+
 619:  \|R_s^\lambda\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}=o(s^{-r}).
 620: \end{equation}
 621: Indeed, after multiplication by \(s^r\), the interior term is dominated by
 622: \(C|x|^r\) and converges pointwise to zero, while for \(j\le r\),
 623: \[
 624:  s^{-j}\int_{|x|>s}|x|^j\lambda(\dd x)
 625:  \le s^{-r}\int_{|x|>s}|x|^r\lambda(\dd x)=o(s^{-r}).
 626: \]
 627: The same last estimate gives
 628: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-mass-smallness}
 629:  \tau_s^\lambda=o(s^{-r}).
 630: \end{equation}
 631: 
 632: Translation preserves mass, so
 633: \[
 634:  V_s^\lambda\ge0,\qquad
 635:  \int V_s^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=\tau_s^\lambda.
 636: \]
 637: Also \(\int B_j^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=0\), by integration of the
 638: derivatives in \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-moment-modes}.  Therefore
 639: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-proxy-normalization}
 640:  \int A_s^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=1.
 641: \end{equation}
 642: Moreover,
 643: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-proxy-positive-lower-bound}
 644:  \inf_yA_s^\lambda(y)
 645:  \ge1-\|C_{r-1,s}\|_\infty-\tau_s^\lambda\longrightarrow1,
 646: \end{equation}
 647: because \(V_s^\lambda\ge0\),
 648: \(\|C_{r-1,s}\|_\infty=O(s^{-1})\) (with value zero when \(r=1\)), and
 649: \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-mass-smallness} holds.  This proves positivity,
 650: including at \(r=1\), and gives a common lower bound such as \(1/2\).
 651: 
 652: We next prove proxy-entropy finiteness without appealing to the covariance
 653: case.  The two-sided stable-density bound in
 654: Proposition~\ref{prop:stable-kernel-input-package} and
 655: \(1+|w+z|\le(1+|w|)(1+|z|)\) give
 656: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-stable-translate-entropy}
 657:  D_{\rm KL}\!\left(p_1(\mathord\cdot-z)\,\middle\|\,p_1\right)
 658:  \le C_{\alpha,d}+(d+\alpha)\log(1+|z|).
 659: \end{equation}
 660: If \(\tau_s^\lambda>0\), write \(V_s^\lambda=\tau_s^\lambda Q_s^\lambda\),
 661: where \(Q_s^\lambda\) is the quotient, relative to \(p_1\), of the
 662: normalized exterior translate mixture.  Convexity of relative entropy and
 663: \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-stable-translate-entropy} imply
 664: \begin{align}
 665:  \int Q_s^\lambda\log Q_s^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}
 666:  &\le\frac1{\tau_s^\lambda}\int_{|x|>s}
 667:  \left\{C_{\alpha,d}+(d+\alpha)\log(1+|x|/s)\right\}
 668:  \lambda(\dd x)\notag\\
 669:  &<\infty,
 670:  \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-normalized-proxy-entropy}
 671: \end{align}
 672: since \(\log(1+|x|/s)\le |x|/s\) and the assumed \(r\)-th moment, with
 673: \(r\ge1\), supplies a finite first moment.  For \(0\le\tau\le1\),
 674: \(\Phi(\tau q)\le\tau q\log(1+q)\); hence
 675: \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-normalized-proxy-entropy}, together with the bound
 676: of the negative part of \(q\log q\), proves
 677: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-potential-entropy-finite}
 678:  \int\Phi(V_s^\lambda)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}<\infty.
 679: \end{equation}
 680: The conclusion is immediate when \(\tau_s^\lambda=0\).
 681: 
 682: For large \(s\), \(|C_{r-1,s}-\tau_s^\lambda|\le1/2\), so
 683: \(A_s^\lambda\le3/2+V_s^\lambda\).  The elementary bound
 684: \[
 685:  (u+v)\log^+(u+v)\le C\{1+v+\Phi(v)\},
 686:  \qquad 0\le u\le3/2,\quad v\ge0,
 687: \]
 688: and \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-potential-entropy-finite} show that
 689: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-proxy-positive-entropy-finite}
 690:  \int A_s^\lambda\log^+A_s^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}<\infty.

The closure route does go through at referee standard. Invertible affine images preserve both quotient bounds; tensor products preserve them via bounded and uniformly continuous normalized derivatives; finite positive mixtures preserve them by convex-weight estimates. I’ll use that stronger closure proposition, so no logistic fallback is needed, and I’ll separate the Gaussian obstruction into its own proposition with the requested bounded-score/additive versus unbounded-score/multiplicative distinction.

 succeeded in 197ms:
The theorem is a specialization of the unnumbered abstract kernel theorem
that precedes it.  For a strictly positive density \(p\), the proof uses only
bounded normalized derivatives through order \(r\), uniform
translation-continuity of the normalized order-\(r\) derivative, and an
order-\(r\) bound on the entropy of a translate of \(p\).  These conditions
hold for all isotropic strictly stable densities, for the Poisson kernel, and
for every multivariate Student density \(c_{\beta,d}(1+|y|^2)^{-\beta/2}\),
\(\beta>d\).  No tail asymptotic or regular variation enters the abstract
proof.  The Gaussian density does not satisfy the bounded normalized-jet
condition: its normalized derivatives are Hermite polynomials, and an
additive common jet need not remain positive.  Thus the Gaussian
first-unmatched-moment theorem recalled above uses a different weighted
Hermite mechanism; it is not presented as a member of the positive
tail-proxy theorem.

 680: The conclusion is immediate when \(\tau_s^\lambda=0\).

 682: For large \(s\), \(|C_{r-1,s}-\tau_s^\lambda|\le1/2\), so
 683: \(A_s^\lambda\le3/2+V_s^\lambda\).  The elementary bound
 684: \[
 685:  (u+v)\log^+(u+v)\le C\{1+v+\Phi(v)\},
 686:  \qquad 0\le u\le3/2,\quad v\ge0,
 687: \]
 688: and \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-potential-entropy-finite} show that
 689: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-proxy-positive-entropy-finite}
 690:  \int A_s^\lambda\log^+A_s^\lambda\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}<\infty.
 691: \end{equation}
 692: Finally \(A_s^\eta\ge1/2\).  The positive part of
 693: \(A_s^\nu\log(A_s^\nu/A_s^\eta)\) is bounded by
 694: \(A_s^\nu\log^+(2A_s^\nu)\), while its negative part is bounded in absolute
 695: integral by \(e^{-1}\int A_s^\eta\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=e^{-1}\).
 696: Thus \(\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)<\infty\), as asserted.
 697: 
 698: It remains to verify, rather than assume, the hypotheses of the abstract
 699: two-background KL perturbation lemma.  Put
 700: \(\epsilon_s=s^{-r}\), take
 701: \[
 702:  (A_{1,s},A_{2,s})=(A_s^\nu,A_s^\eta),\qquad
 703:  (B_{1,s},B_{2,s})=(B_r^\nu,B_r^\eta),
 704:  \qquad (R_{1,s},R_{2,s})=(R_s^\nu,R_s^\eta).
 705: \]
 706: The modes \(B_r^\lambda\) are bounded by
 707: \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-mode-bounds}, and their integrals vanish.  The first
 708: requested background estimate follows directly from the common jet and the
 709: exact tail normalization:
 710: \begin{align}
 711:  \|A_s^\lambda-1\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}
 712:  &\le\|C_{r-1,s}\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}
 713:       +\|V_s^\lambda-\tau_s^\lambda\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}\notag\\
 714:  &\le \sum_{j=1}^{r-1}s^{-j}\|B_j^\lambda\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}
 715:       +2\tau_s^\lambda=o(1).
 716:  \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-background-to-one}
 717: \end{align}
 718: For \(r=1\), the sum is empty and the same proof applies.  For the second
 719: background estimate, the common jet cancels exactly, and therefore
 720: \begin{align}
 721:  \|A_s^\nu-A_s^\eta\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}
 722:  &\le\|V_s^\nu-V_s^\eta\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}
 723:        +|\tau_s^\nu-\tau_s^\eta|\notag\\
 724:  &\le2(\tau_s^\nu+\tau_s^\eta)=o(s^{-r}).
 725:  \label{eq:arbitrary-tail-two-background-separation}
 726: \end{align}
 727: This is the place where retaining the common lower-order jet removes every
 728: interior contribution from the difference of the backgrounds.
 729: 
 730: The proxy lower bound is \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-proxy-positive-lower-bound}.
 731: For the actual quotient, the bounded logarithmic gradient of \(p_1\) gives
 732: \(F_y(z)\ge e^{-L|z|}\), uniformly in \(y\).  Jensen's inequality then yields
 733: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-quotient-positive-lower-bound}
 734:  q_s^\lambda(y)
 735:  \ge\int e^{-L|x|/s}\lambda(\dd x)
 736:  \ge e^{-L\EE_\lambda|X|/s}\longrightarrow1
 737: \end{equation}
 738: uniformly in \(y\).  Thus the actual quotients have a common positive lower
 739: bound.  Their exact split is
 740: \(q_s^\lambda=A_s^\lambda+\epsilon_sB_r^\lambda+R_s^\lambda\),
 741: and \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-remainder-smallness} supplies the required
 742: \(L^1\) and \(L^\infty\) smallness.  Finally,
 743: \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-proxy-positive-entropy-finite} verifies the finite
 744: positive entropy hypothesis.  All hypotheses of the two-background KL
 745: perturbation lemma are now proved.  Its conclusion gives
 746: \[
 747:  D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
 748:  =\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)
 749:  +\frac{s^{-2r}}2\int(B_r^\nu-B_r^\eta)^2
 750:        \dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}+o(s^{-2r}).
 751: \]
 752: By \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-leading-mode},
 753: \(B_r^\nu-B_r^\eta=B_{\Delta_r}\), so the quadratic integral is
 754: \(\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)\).  This proves
 755: \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-decomposition}.  Formula
 756: \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-energy} is a relative \(\Phi\)-entropy and
 757: is therefore nonnegative; the equivalence
 758: \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-criterion} follows immediately.
 759: 
 760: The raw-tail split, its normalization, and the finite-moment dominated
 761: convergence are exactly the steps that survive from the covariance proof.
 762: The new step is the two-background expansion above: it retains the common
 763: lower-order jet inside both positive tail proxies and replaces the
 764: unavailable one-sided \(\Phi\)-energy of a signed tail difference by a
 765: relative \(\Phi\)-energy.  When \(r=2\), \(\eta=\delta_0\), and \(\nu\) is
 766: centred, this energy is \(\int\Phi(V_s^\nu-\tau_s^\nu)\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}\),
 767: which differs from the energy in
 768: Theorem~\ref{thm:stable-law-by-law-decomposition} by \(o(s^{-4})\), as shown
 769:  in \eqref{eq:stable-remove-mass}.
 770: \end{proof}

   1: \begin{corollary}[A sufficient moment condition for defect vanishing]
   2: \label{cor:arbitrary-tail-defect-positive-moment-condition}
   3: Under the hypotheses of
   4: Theorem~\ref{thm:stable-arbitrary-order-law-by-law-decomposition}, suppose in
   5: addition that \(\Delta_r\ne0\) and
   6: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-positive-moment-condition}
   7:  \int_{\RR^d}|x|^{p_{r,\alpha,d}}(\nu+\eta)(\dd x)<\infty.
   8: \end{equation}
   9: Then
  10: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-positive-limit}
  11:  s^{2r}\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)\longrightarrow0.
  12: \end{equation}
  13: This condition includes noncompact pairs of laws.
  14: \end{corollary}
  15: 
  16: \begin{proof}
  17: Theorem~\ref{thm:all-order-stable-first-unmatched-moment} and
  18: \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-decomposition} give, respectively,
  19: \begin{align*}
  20:  D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
  21:  &=\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)s^{-2r}+o(s^{-2r}),\\
  22:  D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
  23:  &=\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)s^{-2r}
  24:    +\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)+o(s^{-2r}).
  25: \end{align*}
  26: Subtracting proves \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-positive-limit}.
  27: 
  28: For a concrete noncompact class, use the finite-support probability blocks
  29: from Lemma~\ref{lem:stable-finite-difference-blocks}.  Convolve \(A_R^+\) and
  30: \(A_R^-\) with the same nondegenerate Gaussian law.  The multinomial formula
  31: shows that the convolved laws still match below order \(r\), while their
  32: order-\(r\) difference is the nonzero difference of the two blocks: every
  33: other term contains a block-moment difference of order below \(r\).  Both
  34: convolved laws are noncompact and have moments of every order, so
  35: \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-positive-moment-condition} applies.
  36: \end{proof}
  37: 
  38: \begin{corollary}[Non-vacuousness of the tail-defect criterion]
  39: \label{cor:arbitrary-tail-defect-nonvacuousness}
  40: If \(d+\alpha>2r\), then for every
  41: \(r\le\rho<p_{r,\alpha,d}\) there are probability laws \(\nu,\eta\) with
  42: finite \(\rho\)-th absolute moments, matching every tensor moment below
  43: order \(r\), and having a finite nonzero order-\(r\) tensor difference, for
  44: which
  45: \begin{equation}\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-unbounded-limsup}
  46:  \limsup_{s\to\infty}s^{2r}\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)=+\infty.
  47: \end{equation}
  48: In particular, vanishing of the explicit nonnegative tail-defect energy is
  49: not automatic under the minimal finite \(r\)-th moment hypothesis.
  50: \end{corollary}
  51: 
  52: \begin{proof}
  53: Use the laws in the sharpness assertion
  54: \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-sharpness}.  They satisfy the hypotheses of the
  55: law-by-law decomposition because \(\rho\ge r\).  Multiplying
  56: \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-decomposition} by \(s^{2r}\) gives
  57: \[
  58:  s^{2r}D_{\rm KL}(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\|p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\eta)
  59:  =\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)
  60:   +s^{2r}\mathcal E_{r,s}(\nu,\eta)+o(1).
  61: \]
  62: The quadratic term is finite and independent of \(s\), whereas the left
  63: side has infinite limsup by \eqref{eq:all-order-stable-sharpness}.  This
  64: forces \eqref{eq:arbitrary-tail-defect-unbounded-limsup}.
  65: \end{proof}

from pathlib import Path
import re
import unittest


ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
INPUT_RE = re.compile(r"\\input\{([^}]+)\}")


def read_tex_source(path: Path) -> str:
    """Read a TeX source together with any local input wrappers."""

    source = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")

    def expand(match: re.Match[str]) -> str:
        child = ROOT / match.group(1)
        if child.suffix == "":
            child = child.with_suffix(".tex")
        return read_tex_source(child)

    return INPUT_RE.sub(expand, source)


class StableSpineManuscriptTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_main_article_keeps_the_restored_article_architecture(self) -> None:
        main = (ROOT / "main.tex").read_text(encoding="utf-8")

        self.assertIn(r"\input{sec_verified_A2_results}", main)
        self.assertIn(r"\input{bibliography_shared}", main)
        self.assertNotIn(r"\input{sec_stable_entropy_spine}", main)
        for article_input in (
            r"\input{sec_entropy_asymptotics}",
            r"\input{sec_entropy_30_eighth_defect_layer}",
            r"\input{sec_gram_space}",
            r"\input{sec_strip_00_poisson_image}",
            r"\input{sec_strip_20_symbol_sampling}",
            r"\input{sec_strip_30_cardinal_observation}",
        ):
            self.assertIn(article_input, main)

    def test_stable_tail_decomposition_is_stated_and_proved(self) -> None:
        theorem_path = ROOT / "sec_verified_A2_results.tex"
        theorem_source = read_tex_source(theorem_path)

        required = (
            r"\label{thm:stable-law-by-law-decomposition}",
            r"\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d}(\Sigma)s^{-4}",
            r"\int_{\{|x|>s\}}",
            r"\frac{p_1^{(\alpha,d)}(y-x/s)}",
            r"\Phi(V_s^\nu)",
            r"o(s^{-4})",
            r"\|R_s\|_\infty+\|R_s\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}=o(s^{-2})",
            r"\int V_s^\nu\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=\tau_s",
            r"No regular-variation, absolute-continuity, or moment hypothesis above order two",
        )
        for text in required:
            self.assertIn(text, theorem_source)

    def test_decomposition_theorem_has_one_source(self) -> None:
        label = r"\label{thm:stable-law-by-law-decomposition}"
        sources = list(ROOT.glob("*.tex"))
        occurrences = sum(
            path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").count(label) for path in sources
        )
        self.assertEqual(occurrences, 1)

    def test_arbitrary_order_proxy_proof_closure_is_explicit(self) -> None:
        source = read_tex_source(ROOT / "sec_verified_A2_results.tex")
        required = (
            "Two-background KL perturbation",
            r"H'(t)",
            r"H''(t)",
            r"H'''(t)",
            r"\|A_s^\lambda-1\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}",
            r"\|A_s^\nu-A_s^\eta\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}",
            r"\label{eq:arbitrary-tail-proxy-positive-entropy-finite}",
            r"\label{cor:arbitrary-tail-defect-positive-moment-condition}",
            r"\label{cor:arbitrary-tail-defect-nonvacuousness}",
        )
        for text in required:
            self.assertIn(text, source)

    def test_abstract_kernel_theorem_and_membership_audit_are_explicit(self) -> None:
        source = read_tex_source(ROOT / "sec_verified_A2_results.tex")
        required = (
            "Abstract positive tail-jet kernel theorem",
            r"\omega_{p,r}(t)",
            "Isotropic stable kernels",
            "Poisson kernel",
            "Multivariate Student kernels",
            "Gaussian obstruction",
            r"\partial^\gamma g/g=(-1)^{|\gamma|}H_\gamma",
            "specialization is exactly Theorem",
        )
        for text in required:
            self.assertIn(text, source)

        self.assertIn(r"\newtheorem*{unnumberedtheorem}{Theorem}",
                      (ROOT / "main.tex").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))

    def test_fixed_scale_robustness_scope_and_rigidity_are_explicit(self) -> None:
        source = read_tex_source(ROOT / "sec_verified_A2_results.tex")
        required = (
            r"((\chi^\nu,\chi^\eta),H)",
            "No relation between",
            "Sharpness of the inner exclusion",
            r"\tau_{s,\chi}^\lambda=\kappa",
            r"(1+\kappa^2)\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)",
            r"\widetilde A_s^{\lambda;\boldsymbol\chi,H}",
            r"\widetilde{\mathcal E}_{r,s}^{\boldsymbol\chi,H}",
            "normalization rules other than",
            "Asymptotic coefficient rigidity inside a prescribed ansatz",
            "not a uniqueness, canonicity, or entropy-minimality theorem",
        )
        for text in required:
            self.assertIn(text, source)

        abstract = (ROOT / "main.tex").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
        intro = read_tex_source(ROOT / "sec_introduction.tex")
        self.assertIn("either additive or multiplicative mass normalization", abstract)
        self.assertIn("not every reasonable", intro)
        self.assertNotIn("robust at its full resolution", intro)
        self.assertNotIn("partial rigidity", abstract + intro + source)

    def test_active_tex_sources_obey_presentation_constraints(self) -> None:
        for path in ROOT.glob("*.tex"):
            source = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
            self.assertLess(
                len(source.splitlines()),
                800,
                f"{path.name} must remain below 800 lines",
            )
            self.assertNotRegex(source, r"(?i)TODO[^\n]*revision")

    def test_literature_audit_names_all_requested_indexes(self) -> None:
        audit = (ROOT / "artifacts" / "literature_check.md").read_text(
            encoding="utf-8"
        )
        for index in ("arXiv", "Crossref", "Semantic Scholar", "zbMATH"):
            self.assertIn(index, audit)
        self.assertIn("Ishige--Kawakami--Michihisa", audit)

    def test_named_problem_audit_records_printed_questions_and_status(self) -> None:
        audit = (ROOT / "artifacts" / "tier2_named_problem_audit.md").read_text(
            encoding="utf-8"
        )
        required = (
            "Johnson, Open Problem 1",
            "corresponding result for the MMSE score",
            "Johnson, Open Problem 4",
            "representation of $D(f \\Vert g_s^{(\\alpha)})$ as an integral",
            "Johnson, Open Problem 6",
            "more general (non-symmetric) families of stable laws",
            "No later source located",
            "Finite-variance Cauchy case proved",
        )
        for text in required:
            self.assertIn(text, audit)

    def test_cauchy_interpolation_integral_representation_is_complete(self) -> None:
        source = read_tex_source(ROOT / "sec_doob_phi_entropy.tex")
        required = (
            r"\label{thm:johnson-cauchy-integral-representation}",
            r"g_q=P_{q+s}=P_q*P_s",
            r"D_{\rm KL}(\mu\|P_s)",
            r"\int_0^\infty \mathcal J_{\mu,s}(q)\dd q",
            r"q=\frac{st}{1-t}",
            r"\frac{s}{(1-t)^2}",
            r"joint lower semicontinuity",
            r"data-processing inequality",
            r"\|u_q-1\|_\infty\longrightarrow0",
            r"Proposition~\ref{prop:compact-window-bregman-identity}",
        )
        for text in required:
            self.assertIn(text, source)

        intro = (ROOT / "sec_introduction.tex").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
        self.assertIn(r"Johnson's integral-representation problem", intro)
        self.assertNotIn(
            r"Theorem~\ref{thm:johnson-cauchy-integral-representation}", intro
        )
        self.assertIn(r"the finite-variance Cauchy statement proved in", intro)

Assume throughout the hypotheses and notation of
Theorem~\ref{thm:stable-arbitrary-order-law-by-law-decomposition}.  For
\(\lambda\in\{\nu,\eta\}\), let
\(\chi^\lambda=(\chi_s^\lambda)_{s>0}\).  Call
\((\boldsymbol\chi,H)=((\chi^\nu,\chi^\eta),H)\) an admissible fixed-scale
tail-jet convention if there are common constants
\(0<a<b<\infty\), independent of \(s\) and \(\lambda\), such that
\[
 0\le\chi_s^\lambda\le1,\qquad
 \chi_s^\lambda(z)=0\quad(|z|\le a),\qquad
 \chi_s^\lambda(z)=1\quad(|z|\ge b),
\]
where every \(\chi_s^\lambda:\RR^d\to[0,1]\) is measurable, and if
\[
 \int H_s\dd\Omega_{\alpha,d}=0,
 \qquad
 \sup_s\bigl(\|H_s\|_\infty+
        \|H_s\|_{L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d})}\bigr)<\infty.
\]
The function \(H_s\) is common to the two laws.  No relation between
\(\chi_s^\nu\) and \(\chi_s^\eta\) is required beyond their common fixed
inner and outer radii.

\paragraph{Sharpness of the inner exclusion.}
The condition that the profiles vanish near the origin is mathematically
necessary at the resolution under study.  Indeed, let a smooth profile
\(\chi\) equal a constant \(\kappa\in(0,1]\) on a neighbourhood of zero and
then rise to one, and let \(\nu,\eta\) be compactly supported, match below
order \(r\), and satisfy \(\Delta_r\ne0\).  For all large \(s\),
the corresponding additively normalized proxy with \(H=0\) satisfies
\[
 \tau_{s,\chi}^\lambda=\kappa,
 \qquad V_{s,\chi}^\lambda=\kappa q_s^\lambda,
 \qquad
 A_s^\lambda=1+C_{r-1,s}+\kappa(q_s^\lambda-1).
\]
Consequently,
\[
 A_s^\nu-A_s^\eta
 =\kappa s^{-r}B_{\Delta_r}+o(s^{-r})
 \quad\hbox{in }L^\infty\cap L^1(\Omega_{\alpha,d}),
 \qquad \inf A_s^\eta\longrightarrow1.
\]
The quadratic KL expansion therefore gives
\[
 D_{\rm KL}(A_s^\nu\|A_s^\eta)
 =\kappa^2\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)s^{-2r}
   +o(s^{-2r}),
\]
whereas Theorem~\ref{thm:all-order-stable-first-unmatched-moment} gives the
actual smoothed entropy coefficient
\(\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)\).  Thus adding the universal quadratic
term to this proxy entropy would give the false coefficient
\((1+\kappa^2)\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d,r}(\Delta_r)\).  The retained raw tail has
absorbed a fixed fraction of the local order-\(r\) energy; the admissible
class boundary is not a proof artifact.

\begin{unnumberedproposition}[Robustness of the positive tail-jet convention]
Define, for \(\lambda\in\{\nu,\eta\}\),
\begin{align*}
 \tau_{s,\chi^\lambda}^\lambda
  &:=\int\chi_s^\lambda(x/s)\lambda(\dd x),\\
 V_{s,\chi^\lambda}^\lambda(y)
  &:=\int\chi_s^\lambda(x/s)F_y^{(\alpha,d)}(x/s)\lambda(\dd x),\\
 A_s^{\lambda;\boldsymbol\chi,H}
  &:=1+C_{r-1,s}+V_{s,\chi^\lambda}^\lambda-
       \tau_{s,\chi^\lambda}^\lambda+s^{-r}H_s,\\
 \widetilde A_s^{\lambda;\boldsymbol\chi,H}
  &:=\frac{1+C_{r-1,s}+V_{s,\chi^\lambda}^\lambda+s^{-r}H_s}
          {1+\tau_{s,\chi^\lambda}^\lambda}.
\end{align*}
These are, respectively, the additive and multiplicative mass
normalizations.  For all sufficiently large \(s\), both pairs are positive
probability densities relative to \(\Omega_{\alpha,d}\).  Their relative
entropies
\[
 \mathcal E_{r,s}^{\boldsymbol\chi,H}(\nu,\eta)
 :=\int A_s^{\eta;\boldsymbol\chi,H}
 \Phi\!\left(
  \frac{A_s^{\nu;\boldsymbol\chi,H}
